**Sources.** Most travel phrases are lines from the listening scripts of the official sample papers, where customers talk to drivers, ticket clerks, hotel staff and shop assistants (203 of 259 phrases cite one). The two native-speaker dialogue collections, which are typed conversations between travellers and service agents, back 92 of them with current usage: 你好, for example, occurs in 12,277 of their 236,674 utterances, mostly as a customer's first word to the agent ([CrossWOZ](https://github.com/thu-coai/CrossWOZ/blob/master/data/crosswoz/train.json.zip), [RiSAWOZ](https://github.com/terryqj0107/RiSAWOZ/blob/master/RiSAWOZ-data/source-data.zip)).

**Tips worth knowing before a trip** (all verified and in the app):

- Call a taxi driver 师傅 (师傅，我去火车站。) ([HSK 4 sample H41002](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v2/H41002.pdf), [CC-CEDICT](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict)).
- Get staff's attention with 你好 or, in restaurants and hotels, 服务员 ([HSK 2 sample H20901](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v1/H20901.pdf)). Do not call a waitress or shop assistant 小姐: CC-CEDICT records the slang sense "prostitute", although older HSK papers still use the word ([CC-CEDICT](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict), [HSK 3 sample H31001](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v1/H31001.pdf)).
- Tipping is not customary in mainland China and may even be refused ([China Daily, 2016](https://www.chinadaily.com.cn/china/2016-08/08/content_26391578.htm)). Sources disagree on service charges (服务费): a teaching site says some upscale restaurants add one, while China Daily's government-services travel page says one is "generally added" to restaurant bills. The app keeps the narrower wording, because its verifier judged the broader one does not match ordinary mainland restaurants ([GoEast Mandarin](https://goeastmandarin.com/ordering-and-checking-the-bill-in-chinese-a-step-by-step-guide/), [China Daily travel page](https://govt.chinadaily.com.cn/s/201905/15/WS5cdbc147498e079e68020f1e/tipping.html)).
- Bargain at markets, street stalls and tourist stalls, not in modern malls, where prices are fixed ([Yoyo Chinese](https://yoyochinese.com/blog/8-Tips-Youll-Need-Shop-Successfully-China), [HSK 3 sample H31003](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v3/H31003.pdf)).
- When paying by phone, either you scan the shop's code (我扫你) or the cashier scans yours (你扫我) ([BCT A](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTA.pdf), [LTL](https://ltl-school.com/wechat-pay-for-foreigners/)).
- Carry your destination in Chinese characters and show it to the driver (confidence medium: teaching and travel sites only) ([GoEast Mandarin](https://goeastmandarin.com/essential-vocabulary-and-tips-for-taking-a-taxi-in-mandarin-chinese/)).
- A "vegetable" dish can still contain lard, meat stock or chicken bouillon powder (鸡精, made from chicken meat, eggs, chicken bones and MSG), so ask about each dish ([现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt), [LTL](https://ltl-school.com/vegetarian-vegan-china/)).
- Emergency numbers: 110 police, 119 fire, 120 ambulance ([Android emergency-number list](https://github.com/LineageOS/android_packages_services_Telephony/blob/lineage-24.0/ecc/input/eccdata.txt)).

**Hidden** (**UNVERIFIED**, 4 phrases and 2 tips):

- 劳驾，去中国银行怎么走？ **UNVERIFIED** (refuted): see 3.1.
- 调料里有放肉吗？ ("Is there meat in the seasoning?") **UNVERIFIED** (refuted): 调料 means seasonings such as oil, salt, soy sauce, vinegar, scallion and ginger ([现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt)), so asking whether meat was put in them sounds odd. The app teaches 这个菜有没有肉？ and 请不要用猪油和肉汤 instead.
- 我就在书店门口等你。 ("I'm waiting at the bookshop entrance") **UNVERIFIED**: the quote is in an official paper ([HSK 3 sample H31001](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v1/H31001.pdf)), but no second, independent source was found.
- 有，您要几张？ ("Yes, how many do you want?") **UNVERIFIED**: likewise only in one paper ([HSK 2 sample H20901](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v1/H20901.pdf)).
- Tip "move to the door when you hear 下一站就要到了…" **UNVERIFIED**: one HSK 4 reading passage, no independent source ([HSK 4 sample H41003](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v3/H41003.pdf)). The announcement itself is a verified phrase.
- Tip on addressing hotel staff **UNVERIFIED** in its hotel version; the same advice (你好 or 服务员, not 小姐) is shown through two verified tips in the greetings and restaurant scenes.

**Decision.** Teach what a traveller says and hears in service exchanges, from the exam papers first, with frequency checks in native dialogue. **Today** the app shows 255 phrases, 48 tips and 16 dialogues in 10 scenes ([travel.json](../data/situations/travel.json), [dialogues.json](../data/generated/dialogues.json)). **Later:** the research found no source for the short fast-food question "for here or to go?" (the app has only the longer 您是在这儿用餐，还是给您打包带走？) or for anything stronger than 我对花生严重过敏 ("I'm severely allergic to peanuts"), which rests on Tatoeba alone and should be checked by a native speaker before you rely on it. What to do after losing your passport (police report, exit-entry office, embassy) was not researched; the app only has the sentence 我的护照丢了. 18 travel phrases were adapted from a sourced sentence by swapping in a word, for example 火车晚点了 from 飞机晚点了 in an HSK 5 sample ([H51001](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v1/H51001.pdf)); their usage note says "adapted", and a native speaker should read them. How hot "mild" (微辣) is in your partner's family's province is unknown.

### 3.3 Travel facts that change (checked 2 November 2026)

The 31 facts in [facts.json](../data/reference/facts.json) each carry the date they were checked, a page to re-check (an official one, except Google's phone-number data for the emergency numbers and the GFWList for the hosting card) and their sources. All 31 are verified. 11 passed the first verifier. The other 20 had either been rated "unverifiable" by that verifier, who could not open the official sites and had no searches left (16), or been confirmed by it while the research still marked them unverified (4); they went through a second check on 2 November 2026: a checker with fresh web searches, then a skeptic who tried to refute each answer. That check corrected 18, mostly by adding dates and newer figures, narrowing claims or dropping lines no source supported, and confirmed 2. Official Chinese and Dutch government pages could not be opened directly and were read through search-result summaries; law texts and the Dutch travel advice were also read from copies on GitHub. For the 20 re-checked facts, `verification.recheck` in the data says what was read and how.

| Topic | As checked on 2 Nov 2026 | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Visa-free entry | With an ordinary Dutch passport you need no visa for business, tourism, visiting family or friends, exchange visits and transit, for up to 30 days per entry. Work, study and journalism still need a visa. The Netherlands has been covered since 1 Dec 2023 (15 days at first, 30 days since 30 Nov 2024). | [embassy notice, Nov 2023](https://nl.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/ls/202311/t20231124_11186562.htm), [embassy notice, Nov 2024](https://nl.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/ls/202411/t20241123_11531814.htm) |
| Until when | Until 24:00 Beijing time on 31 Dec 2026 (embassy notice of 4 Nov 2025). No extension for the Netherlands had been announced by 2 Nov 2026; the extension to 31 Dec 2027 announced on 20 May 2026 covers Russia only. Until a new notice appears, assume you need a visa for any entry from 1 Jan 2027, and ask the embassy if a stay that starts in December would run into 2027. | [embassy notice, 4 Nov 2025](https://nl.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/ls/zytz/202511/t20251104_11746916.htm), [Shanghai government, 20 May 2026](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-VisaLatest/20260520/0b86f04686c84c4a8bdca4f77dbee1eb.html) |
| Counting the 30 days | From the day after you arrive, 30 calendar days in a row: arrive on 1 March, leave by the end of 31 March. There is currently no limit on the number of entries, but what you do must match the purpose of your visit; the passport must be valid for the whole trip. Overstaying brings a warning or, in serious cases, CNY 500 a day up to CNY 10,000, or 5-15 days' detention (Art. 78 of the Exit and Entry Administration Law). | [embassy FAQ, 10 Nov 2025](https://nl.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/ls/fhqz/202511/t20251110_11750419.htm), [Beijing FAQ, 22 May 2026](https://wb.beijing.gov.cn/en/express/202605/t20260522_4661952.html), [law text in Chinese, GitHub copy](<https://github.com/LawRefBook/Laws/blob/5057151c9258a98a35dafc06fecad4867ada81de/行政法/出境入境管理法(2012-06-30).md>) |
| 240-hour transit | The fallback if the 30-day rule lapses. The Netherlands is among 57 countries (since 20 Aug 2026). You need an ordinary passport valid for at least 3 months and a confirmed onward ticket to a third country or region within 240 hours; you enter through one of 65 designated ports in 24 provincial-level regions and stay within the permitted areas. | [National Immigration Administration (NIA), 20 Aug 2026](https://en.nia.gov.cn/n147418/n147463/c183412/content.html), [State Council / Xinhua, 20 Aug 2026](https://english.www.gov.cn/news/202608/20/content_WS6a866e4fc6d00ca5f9a0cb76.html) |
| Arrival card | Can be filled in online since 20 Nov 2025, free: on the NIA website, in the NIA 12367 app or its WeChat and Alipay mini programs. The paper card still exists. The NIA warns of scam sites that imitate the service and charge a fee. | [NIA](https://en.nia.gov.cn/n147418/n147463/c191530/content.html), [embassy notice, 26 Nov 2025](https://nl.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/ls/zytz/202511/t20251126_11760675.htm) |
| Staying with family | A hotel registers you with the police. In a private home, you or your host must register you with the local police within 24 hours of moving in; that is the law's wording (入住后二十四小时内), re-read on 2 Nov 2026; the 24 hours start when you arrive at that home, also if you first stay in a hotel. The registration card now says so, and the Dutch travel-advice card, which quotes "within 24 hours of arrival", adds the law's wording ([data/reference/facts.json](../data/reference/facts.json)). A warning and a fine of up to CNY 2,000 are possible (Arts. 39 and 76). Online registration has the same legal effect and works nationwide since 21 Sep 2026 (NIA 12367 app or mini programs); the host normally files the first registration. | [Exit and Entry Administration Law](https://english.www.gov.cn/archive/laws_regulations/2014/09/22/content_281474988553532.htm), [law text, Chinese embassy in the US](https://us.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/lsfw/zj/notice/201307/t20130702_4899883.htm), [law text in Chinese, GitHub copy](<https://github.com/LawRefBook/Laws/blob/5057151c9258a98a35dafc06fecad4867ada81de/行政法/出境入境管理法(2012-06-30).md>), [NIA, online registration](https://en.nia.gov.cn/n147418/n147468/c237295/content.html) |
| Your passport | Foreigners aged 16 or over must carry their passport and show it when the police ask (Art. 38). It is also your train ticket. | [law text, Chinese embassy in the US](https://us.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/lsfw/zj/notice/201307/t20130702_4899883.htm) |
| Paying with a foreign card | Alipay and WeChat Pay accept Visa and Mastercard (since July 2023). After you verify your passport in the app, the caps are USD 5,000 per payment and USD 50,000 a year (announced 1 Mar 2024). WeChat Pay: free up to RMB 200 per payment, 3% above; if you link an international card for the first time by 31 Dec 2026, the 3% is waived for 90 days on up to RMB 1,000 a day. No official Alipay fee page was found; guides give the same RMB 200 / 3% rule. | [Beijing government, Jul 2023](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/livinginbeijing/finance/mobilepaymentlist/202307/t20230722_3205013.html), [State Council, 1 Mar 2024](https://english.www.gov.cn/news/202403/01/content_WS65e1dacdc6d0868f4e8e487b.html), [Tencent](https://www.tencent.com/tencent-announces-three-initiatives-to-make-inbound-payments-for-international-visitors-easier-than-ever-during-apec-2026/) |
| Cash | Renminbi cash is legal tender; businesses with staffed checkouts must accept it and keep change (rules in force since 1 Feb 2026). Carry some small notes as a backup. Withdrawals with overseas cards are generally limited to RMB 3,000 per transaction. | [PBOC, NDRC and NFRA announcement](https://www.gov.cn/zhengce/zhengceku/202512/content_7055739.htm), [Shanghai government, Sept 2026](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-Individuals-BankingandPayments-Payments/20260921/c2c94d06a0454c8fa05386b80d0ae865.html) |
| Trains | Register on 12306 (website or app, in English) with your passport and have it verified before you buy: online since 28 Nov 2023, but a manual review can take several days. Tickets are electronic: you enter and board with the passport you booked with. Whether automatic gates read passports varies by station; if a gate refuses yours, use the staffed lane 人工通道. Ticket checking commonly closes 3-5 minutes before departure (press report, Oct 2026); guides advise arriving 40-60 minutes early, which is advice, not a rule. | [China Railway, 30 Nov 2023](http://www.china-railway.com.cn/xwzx/ywsl/202311/t20231130_131696.html), [12306 FAQ](https://www.12306.cn/en/faq.html), [Shanghai government](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-Transportation/20240110/f69efb3643094385a0efeb62f594200f.html), [Stockstar](https://wap.stockstar.com/detail/IG2026102100036367) |
| Internet | Google, Facebook, Instagram, WhatsApp, YouTube, X and many Dutch and international news sites are always blocked (Dutch travel advice, last changed 28 Aug 2026). The community block list GFWList (31 Oct 2026) lists vercel.app, where this app is hosted, so install the app and let it download everything before you leave. | [Dutch travel advice](https://www.nederlandwereldwijd.nl/reisadvies/china), [GFWList](https://github.com/gfwlist/gfwlist/blob/master/gfwlist.txt), [Vercel](https://vercel.com/kb/guide/accessing-vercel-hosted-sites-from-mainland-china) |
| Apps | Install before you go: Alipay and WeChat (paying), Railway 12306 (trains), NIA 12367 (arrival card and registration). For taxis, the Hunan government names DiDi's international app and the taxi mini programs in Alipay and WeChat (confidence low for this part). | [Shanghai government, Sept 2026](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-Individuals-BankingandPayments-Payments/20260921/c2c94d06a0454c8fa05386b80d0ae865.html), [Hunan government, Jun 2026](https://www.enghunan.gov.cn/hneng/Services/Live/Transportation/UrbanTransport/202606/t20260608_33692321.html) |
| Emergency numbers | 110 police, 119 fire, 120 ambulance; dial the three digits directly. 122 for traffic accidents: **UNVERIFIED**. Whether 112 dialled on a Dutch phone reaches Chinese emergency services: **UNVERIFIED**, so use 110, 119 or 120. | [libphonenumber](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/libphonenumber/master/resources/ShortNumberMetadata.xml), [Android emergency-number list](https://github.com/LineageOS/android_packages_services_Telephony/blob/lineage-24.0/ecc/input/eccdata.txt) |

The other facts on the page: no routine health declaration form on entry since 1 Nov 2023, although travellers with symptoms of an infectious disease must report them to customs ([Beijing government](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/latest/news/202311/t20231101_3292824.html)); the Dutch embassy and consulates with their phone numbers and consular districts (3.7) ([NederlandWereldwijd open data, copy](https://github.com/NWW-team/Reisadvies-Reviewer/blob/253b9b3ae4d6a021e1ba5d00a8ce34a23c3ec049/data/posten/CHN.xml)); the Dutch travel advice, colour code yellow ([Dutch travel advice](https://www.nederlandwereldwijd.nl/reisadvies/china)); the 2026 State Council provisions on exit and entry, in force since 15 Sep 2026, under which you must state your true purpose and officers may ask for documents and electronic data ([Xinhua](https://www.news.cn/20260731/64967b5190314a548e70e6f2ac3c079a/c.html)); three low-confidence cards: the legal status of VPNs, on which the app gives no advice ([Cyberspace Administration of China](https://www.cac.gov.cn/2017-01/23/c_1120366809.htm)); roaming and eSIMs, where China is outside the EU roaming area and the claims about getting round the blocks come only from eSIM sellers ([KPN](https://www.kpn.com/mobiel-abonnement/bundels/buitenland/wereld), [Vodafone](https://www.vodafone.nl/abonnement/buitenland)); Alipay's TourCard, reported discontinued for new users from 29 May 2026 by travel blogs only ([chinaguidelines.com](https://chinaguidelines.com/en/posts/tour-card)); and a medium-confidence card on foreign e-wallets, where PayPal works at WeChat Pay merchants only for US users since 11 Aug 2026 ([PayPal](https://newsroom.paypal-corp.com/2026-08-11-PayPal-World-Enables-US-Travelers-to-Pay-Like-a-Local-at-Weixin-Pay-Merchants-Across-China)).

**Where sources disagree:**

- *Counting the days.* The embassy's notice of Nov 2024 says "no more than 30 days from the date of entry"; its FAQ of 10 Nov 2025 counts "from the next day of entry". The app uses the FAQ, because it is newer and more specific and the Beijing government's FAQ of 22 May 2026 says the same ([embassy notice](https://nl.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/ls/202411/t20241123_11531814.htm), [embassy FAQ](https://nl.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/ls/fhqz/202511/t20251110_11750419.htm), [Beijing FAQ](https://wb.beijing.gov.cn/en/express/202605/t20260522_4661952.html)).
- *Passports at automatic gates.* Shanghai's and Beijing's guides (2024) say passports work at automatic gates or machines; 12306's 2020 e-ticket notice sends passport holders to staffed channels; 2026 guides say it varies by station ([Shanghai government](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-Transportation/20240110/f69efb3643094385a0efeb62f594200f.html), [Beijing government](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/specials/aguidetoworkingandlivinginchinaasbusinessexpatriates2024/dailylifeservices/202404/t20240424_3635102.html), [12306 notice](https://www.12306.cn/mormhweb/zxdtzz/202006/t20200619_597210.html)). The app says: try the gate, otherwise use the staffed lane.
- *WeChat Pay's offer for new card users.* The Beijing government gives 60 days, Tencent's own newer release of 27 May 2026 gives 90 days; the app uses Tencent's ([Beijing government, Mar 2026](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/travellinginbeijing/essentials/inboundtourismfacilitationmeasures/202603/t20260326_4566766.html), [Tencent](https://www.tencent.com/tencent-announces-three-initiatives-to-make-inbound-payments-for-international-visitors-easier-than-ever-during-apec-2026/)).
- *How much you can pay before verifying your passport.* Figures conflict (USD 2,000, RMB 15,000 and others, some undated); the app gives only the official caps and says to go by the limit the app shows you ([State Council](https://english.www.gov.cn/news/202403/01/content_WS65e1dacdc6d0868f4e8e487b.html)).
- *240-hour countries.* Some third-party pages still say 55; the NIA page of 20 Aug 2026 says 57, and the app uses the NIA ([NIA](https://en.nia.gov.cn/n147418/n147463/c183412/content.html)).

**How to re-check before a trip** (from the [README](../README.md)): open each fact's re-check page (the app's *Travel facts* page links it as "Re-check here"), compare, edit `answer`, `checked_on` and `sources` (with the new access date) in [facts.json](../data/reference/facts.json), then run `npm run build && npm run validate` and commit. A fact that fails a later check gets `"status": "unverified"`: the app then lists only its question and its official page under *Not double-checked yet*, never the answer, and the validator fails if such an entry carries an answer ([content.mjs](../scripts/lib/content.mjs), [validate.mjs](../scripts/validate.mjs)). On 2 November 2026 that list is empty.

**Decision.** Travel facts are dated reference cards, kept apart from the study material, and only verified ones are shown, each with its check date and re-check page. **Today** the *Travel facts* page shows all 31, grouped by topic, each with "Checked 2026-11-02", a "Re-check here" link and its sources ([facts.js](../app/src/views/facts.js)). **Later:** the research recommended that cards expire by themselves (the visa-free card on 31 Dec 2026); the app does not do that yet, so the dates must be watched by hand. Re-check the visa policy on the embassy's notices page in November-December 2026 and before booking a trip in 2027 ([embassy notices](https://nl.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/ls/zytz/)).

### 3.4 Family

| Scene | Phrases shown / researched | Tips shown / researched | Dialogues |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Meeting your partner's parents: what to call them and what to say | 30 / 30 | 8 / 8 | 2 |
| First visit: arriving and giving gifts | 17 / 17 | 6 / 6 | 2 |
| Family dinner: eating, toasting, thanking | 28 / 28 | 8 / 8 | 2 |
| Small talk with elders and answering compliments | 30 / 30 | 5 / 5 | 2 |
| Chinese New Year (春节 / 过年) | 29 / 31 | 7 / 8 | 2 |
| Red envelopes (红包) | 17 / 20 | 9 / 9 | 0 |
| Other festivals, birthdays, weddings and condolences | 25 / 25 | 9 / 9 | 0 |
| **Total** | **176 / 181** | **52 / 53** | **10** |
| Kinship chart (*Family terms*) | 102 / 102 terms | 19 / 19 rules | – |

**Sources.** The family phrases come from dialogues in the official papers (叔叔，您…, 阿姨，您…), from the list of speaking functions in Hanban's 2002 syllabus for foreign students at Chinese universities, which has separate entries for greeting, farewell, gifts and modesty ([function list, greetings](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%201/%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E/2002-%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E-%E9%AB%98%E7%AD%89%E5%AD%A6%E6%A0%A1%E5%A4%96%E5%9B%BD%E7%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E7%94%9F%E6%B1%89%E8%AF%AD%E6%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E9%95%BF%E6%9C%9F%E8%BF%9B%E4%BF%AE%EF%BC%89-%E9%99%84%E4%BB%B6.pdf#page=197), [function list, modesty](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%201/%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E/2002-%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E-%E9%AB%98%E7%AD%89%E5%AD%A6%E6%A0%A1%E5%A4%96%E5%9B%BD%E7%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E7%94%9F%E6%B1%89%E8%AF%AD%E6%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E9%95%BF%E6%9C%9F%E8%BF%9B%E4%BF%AE%EF%BC%89-%E9%99%84%E4%BB%B6.pdf#page=237)), from the two dictionaries, and from CCTV news transcripts for festival wishes and dates ([archive](https://github.com/DuckBurnIncense/xin-wen-lian-bo)). The kinship chart rests on CC-CEDICT, the 现代汉语词典 and the kinship calculator relationship.js, which has a northern and a Cantonese variant ([main list](https://github.com/mumuy/relationship/blob/35c44427befb6fd59e16f583b5b92a680a7948db/src/module/data/main.js), [northern](https://github.com/mumuy/relationship/blob/35c44427befb6fd59e16f583b5b92a680a7948db/src/locale/north.js), [Cantonese](https://github.com/mumuy/relationship/blob/35c44427befb6fd59e16f583b5b92a680a7948db/src/locale/guangdong.js)). Etiquette depends more on informal web sources here than elsewhere: 41 of the 53 tips cite a search result (Chinese press, Zhihu, teaching sites), and 37 of the 52 shown tips carry confidence medium or low.

**Kinship chart.** 102 terms in four groups (father's side 25, mother's side 21, siblings, cousins, children and grandchildren 26, spouses and in-laws 30) and 19 rules, all verified (71 terms confirmed, 31 corrected). For each term the app says whether you use it to call the person or only to talk about them, and gives regional variants ([kinship.js](../app/src/views/kinship.js)); the terms are also a study deck.

**Hidden** (**UNVERIFIED**, 5 phrases and 1 tip), all for lack of an independent source:

- 给您拜个晚年！ ("A belated Happy New Year to you!") **UNVERIFIED**: not found in the CCTV transcripts 2022-2026, the HSK and YCT papers, Tatoeba, CC-CEDICT, the 现代汉语词典 or the learner corpora.
- 学业进步！, 学习进步！ and 快快长大！ (New Year wishes for children) **UNVERIFIED**: not in the local corpora, and the search on children's red-envelope wishes found no source for them; only articles with birthday wishes for children use similar lines.
- 手气最佳 (the "luckiest draw" label on a WeChat group red packet) **UNVERIFIED**: only the researcher's press source.
- Tip on New Year taboos (avoiding unlucky words, not sweeping on the first day, 碎碎平安 when something breaks) **UNVERIFIED**: only partial support.

None of these wordings appears in the explanations of verified items any more: 学业进步, 学习进步, 快快长大, 手气最佳, 我司 and 顺颂商祺 were removed from the tips and notes that quoted them, and the validator now fails if a shown text quotes a hidden phrase ([scripts/validate.mjs](../scripts/validate.mjs)). The expression 拜个晚年 stays in the two tips on New Year timing, because their own sources attest it (a People's Daily article titled 给您“拜个晚年”, Baidu Baike and the 人文松江大辞典 via The Paper); only the full sentence 给您拜个晚年！ lacked a second source, so its phrase card stays hidden.

**Decision.** Teach the forms of address, the visit, the meal and the festivals with phrases from official papers and dictionaries, and give the etiquette as tips with their sources and regional notes, telling you to ask your partner where customs differ by family. **Today** the app shows 176 phrases, 52 tips, 10 dialogues and the kinship chart ([family.json](../data/situations/family.json), [kinship.json](../data/reference/kinship.json), [dialogues.json](../data/generated/dialogues.json)). **Later:** the research did not settle the kinship words specific to your partner's province, who gives the 改口费 and how much, who pays when you eat out with the parents, and the customs of the Winter Solstice and the Double Ninth festival; the hidden wordings above need either a second source or removal from the tips.

| Scene | Phrases shown / researched | Tips shown / researched | Dialogues |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Titles and forms of address | 19 / 19 | 6 / 6 | 0 |
| Introductions | 27 / 27 | 5 / 5 | 2 |
| Exchanging contacts | 22 / 22 | 5 / 5 | 2 |
| Meetings: opening, running and closing | 32 / 32 | 2 / 3 | 2 |
| Polite requests, thanks, apologies and soft disagreement | 33 / 33 | 6 / 6 | 2 |
| Scheduling meetings and calls | 33 / 33 | 2 / 3 | 2 |
| WeChat and email: openers, closers and conventions | 24 / 27 | 5 / 5 | 0 |
| **Total** | **190 / 193** | **31 / 33** | **10** |

**Sources.** The business test papers supply workplace dialogues, letters and short texts on etiquette ([BCT A](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTA.pdf), [BCT B](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTB.pdf), [BCT B, older edition](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/BCTB0001.pdf)), with the HSK papers, the vocational Chinese standard T/ISCLT 002-2023 ([standard](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/more/TISCLT%20002-2023%20%E8%81%8C%E4%B8%9A%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E8%83%BD%E5%8A%9B%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86.pdf)) and the 现代汉语词典. The native-speaker dialogues decided between wordings: the dictionary lists 费神, 劳神 and 有劳 as polite formulas for asking a favour, but they occur 0 times in the dialogue collections and the HSK and BCT papers, against about 2,100 times for 麻烦您 or 麻烦你, so the app teaches 麻烦您 and marks the others as formal and rare ([现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt), [CrossWOZ](https://github.com/thu-coai/CrossWOZ/blob/master/data/crosswoz/train.json.zip), [RiSAWOZ](https://github.com/terryqj0107/RiSAWOZ/blob/master/RiSAWOZ-data/source-data.zip)).

**Hidden** (**UNVERIFIED**, 3 phrases and 2 tips):

- 顺颂商祺 (a closing formula for business letters) **UNVERIFIED**: not found as a whole in any source read; Chinese email guides use the variant 顺祝商祺, which is in the app.
- 敬上 ("yours sincerely") and 我司 ("our company") **UNVERIFIED**: only CC-CEDICT supports them; neither is a headword in the 现代汉语词典 or the 新华 data, and the official letters write 我公司 or sign with a name ([CC-CEDICT](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict), [BCT A](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTA.pdf), [BCT B](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTB.pdf)). The verified tip on 贵/我 pairs still mentions 我司 in passing.
- Tips on the order of handshakes (meetings scene) and on phone etiquette (scheduling scene) **UNVERIFIED**: each rests on one official reading text with no independent source in that check ([BCT B](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTB.pdf), [BCT B, older edition](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/BCTB0001.pdf)). The same advice is in the app through two verified tips in the introductions and contacts scenes, which a different verifier confirmed.

**Decision.** Teach the address forms, introductions, meeting language, requests, scheduling and written formulas from the business papers, checked for current use in native dialogue. **Today** the app shows 190 phrases, 31 tips and 10 dialogues ([work.json](../data/situations/work.json), [dialogues.json](../data/generated/dialogues.json)). **Later:** no source was found for several things you may need: what to write in a WeChat friend request, how fast to reply on WeChat at work, replies to 您贵姓 such as 免贵姓王, replies to 请多关照, how English names are used in international companies, and whether surname + 小姐 is still usual in offices. Some meeting, scheduling and writing phrases were assembled by a verifier from attested parts, without the exact sentence being found (confidence medium), and should be read by a native speaker.

### 3.6 The etiquette that matters most

Twelve findings, chosen for a first visit to your partner's family and for work. All are verified and in the app unless marked otherwise.

1. **Before marriage, call your partner's parents 叔叔 and 阿姨.** Greet them with 叔叔阿姨好; use 伯父/伯母 instead if they are older than your own parents. Speak to them with 您 and never by their names ([HSK 3 syllabus and sample](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%8F%8A%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/HSK3.pdf), [现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt), [maigoo](https://www.maigoo.com/goomai/4zAMODA4.html), [ChinesePod](https://chinesepod.com/lesson/dinner-with-the-girlfriends-parents)). The verifier corrected the research here: surname + title (王阿姨) is for other older acquaintances, not for the partner's parents.
2. **After the wedding, change to 爸 and 妈 (改口).** This usually happens at the wedding tea ceremony (改口茶), where the parents give you money called 改口费. Don't start on your own before that, but follow the parents if they invite it earlier, for example after the engagement ([CC-CEDICT](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict), [现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt), [Toutiao](https://www.toutiao.com/zixun/7550172993985742889/), [Marie Claire Taiwan](https://www.marieclaire.com.tw/relationship/secret-talk/83492)). Who gives 改口费 and how much varies by region and family ([huichenglawyer.com](https://www.huichenglawyer.com/pufaku/83209.html)); confidence medium. For the rest of the family, call each relative what your partner calls them (随配偶称呼). 公公/婆婆 and 岳父/岳母 are words for talking about parents-in-law, not for calling them ([Speaking of China](https://www.speakingofchina.com/mandarin-love/learn-mandarin-chinese-what-to-call-chinese-family-for-western-women-married-to-chinese-men/), [ETtoday, 2026](https://www.ettoday.net/news/20260517/3165993.htm)); confidence medium, and calling your partner's younger siblings by name is an inference.
3. **Gifts: bring something for both parents, hand it over with both hands, and expect a polite refusal.** Typical gifts are fruit, tea, health products and specialties from home, so Dutch ones fit; alcohol or cigarettes only if the father drinks or smokes (confidence low: this rests on consumer sites and a forum; [maigoo](https://m.maigoo.com/top/420725.html), [V2EX](https://www.v2ex.com/t/768918)). Hosts protest (来就来嘛，还带什么东西！, 你太客气了！); this is ritual, so keep offering warmly (一点心意, 您就收下吧！) until they accept ([现代汉语词典, 客气](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/master/XDHYCD7th.txt#L29625), [function list, gifts](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%201/%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E/2002-%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E-%E9%AB%98%E7%AD%89%E5%AD%A6%E6%A0%A1%E5%A4%96%E5%9B%BD%E7%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E7%94%9F%E6%B1%89%E8%AF%AD%E6%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E9%95%BF%E6%9C%9F%E8%BF%9B%E4%BF%AE%EF%BC%89-%E9%99%84%E4%BB%B6.pdf#page=201)). A gift is traditionally put aside and opened after the guest has left ([Cultural Atlas](https://culturalatlas.sbs.com.au/chinese-culture/chinese-culture-etiquette), [LTL](https://ltl-school.com/chinese-gift-giving/)). Whether younger Chinese now open gifts in front of the giver: **UNVERIFIED**.
4. **Gifts to avoid.** A clock (送钟 sounds like 送终, attending a dying elder), pears (梨 like 离 "part"), umbrellas (伞 like 散 "break up"), shoes (鞋 like 邪 "evil"), a green hat (it implies an unfaithful partner), knives or scissors, white or black wrapping, and anything in fours (四 like 死 "death"); pairs and even numbers are preferred ([People's Daily](https://en.people.cn/n/2015/0202/c90782-8844413.html), [China Daily](http://www.chinadaily.com.cn/china/2015-02/24/content_19641423.htm), [China Daily, green hat](https://www.chinadaily.com.cn/life/2008-12/29/content_7349364.htm), [The Beijinger](https://www.thebeijinger.com/blog/2020/10/19/five-things-you-should-never-give-gift-china)). The sources disagree about watches, so the app says to avoid them for elders. The sources date from 2008-2020, and younger city people may be relaxed about some of these; for a first visit, play safe.
5. **Toasting.** When you clink glasses with an elder, hold yours lower than theirs. An official business-test answer key gives 敬酒时杯子放低 ("keep your glass low when toasting"); the detail "lower than theirs" comes from a Chinese etiquette article, so confidence is medium ([BCT B, listening item 41](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTB.pdf), [dxsbb](https://www.dxsbb.com/news/713.html)). 干杯 literally means "drain the cup" ([现代汉语词典, 干杯](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt#L16659)), but you need not empty every glass: say 我干了，您随意 ("I'll finish mine, you drink as you like") if you drink; if you don't, give a reason (我今天开车来的，不能喝酒, "I drove today") and toast with tea, 我以茶代酒，敬您一杯. Avoid 我就喝一点 ("just a little"), which invites endless top-ups ([dxsbb](https://www.dxsbb.com/news/713.html), [HSK Lord](https://hsklord.com/chinese-plus/wine-dining), [现代汉语词典, 随意](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt#L49764)). Confidence medium; how hard hosts press drinks differs by region and was not researched.
6. **Refusing food, being served, sitting down.** Hosts urge you to eat and drink more; decline with a short reason (不了，我真的饱了) rather than a bare no ([现代汉语词典, 劝酒](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt#L43176), [YCT 3 sample](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/YCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v5/Y31005.pdf)). When they put food into your bowl, accept some and thank them; if serving chopsticks (公筷) are on the table, use them, which many cities have promoted since 2020 ([Beijing News](https://www.bjnews.com.cn/detail/159176659215913.html), [People's Daily group](https://paper.people.com.cn/zgcsb/html/2020-03/30/content_1979000.htm)). Let the host seat you: the seat facing the door is the seat of honour, the elders sit down first, and you start eating after the most senior person has picked up their chopsticks (您先请) ([现代汉语词典, 上座](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/master/XDHYCD7th.txt#L45580), [China Daily](https://language.chinadaily.com.cn/a/202405/24/WS66505c0da31082fc043c8fed.html), [The Paper](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_25151316)). Guides differ on whether that seat goes to the host or the guest of honour, so follow the host.
7. **Red envelopes (红包).** They go from older to younger and from married to unmarried ([LTL](https://ltl-school.com/red-envelope/), [现代汉语词典, 压岁钱](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt#L59614)). As an unmarried partner on a first New Year visit you are not expected to give the elders one; many visitors give each young child an envelope with the same round, even sum (one Zhihu answer: 50-200 yuan is common for minors), and the parents may give you one as a first-meeting present, 见面礼 ([Zhihu](https://www.zhihu.com/question/577959282), [现代汉语词典, 见面礼](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt#L25463)). Avoid amounts with a 4 and the sum 250 (二百五 means "idiot"), use crisp new notes, accept an envelope with both hands and don't open it in front of the giver ([LTL](https://ltl-school.com/red-envelope/), [ChinesePod](http://chinesepod.com/blog/chinese-new-year-red-envelopes-give-receive-hongbao-like-local), [People's Daily, 2026](http://finance.people.com.cn/n1/2026/0213/c1004-40665447.html)). WeChat red packets, capped at 200 yuan each, need a mainland Chinese bank card, so with only a Dutch card ask your partner to send one or give a paper envelope ([Shanghai government](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-BankingServices/20231215/fe3a293d065f40e0a7f740aa88edb050.html), [LTL](https://ltl-school.com/wechat-pay-for-foreigners/), [The Paper](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32613189)). Never give red envelopes or mooncakes to public officials ([CC-CEDICT, 红包](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict), [CCTV, 27 Sep 2025](https://github.com/DuckBurnIncense/xin-wen-lian-bo/blob/31df1d3c9cd2c5fbb766939513968cc8bcf11511/news/20250927.md)).
8. **New Year and the other festivals.** The next Spring Festival is Saturday 6 February 2027 (Year of the Goat; New Year's Eve Friday 5 February); the 2027 public-holiday arrangement had not been published on 2 November 2026, so these dates were computed from the lunar calendar ([holiday-cn 2027 file, still empty](https://github.com/NateScarlet/holiday-cn/blob/2fa4d1e5fb471bc89bdabeb630c141fee6201575/2027.json), [Astronomy Engine](https://github.com/cosinekitty/astronomy), [CCTV, 17 Feb 2026, for the 2026 festival](https://github.com/DuckBurnIncense/xin-wen-lian-bo/blob/31df1d3c9cd2c5fbb766939513968cc8bcf11511/news/20260217.md)). Until New Year's Eve you wish people an early New Year (拜个早年); from New Year's Day you say 过年好 or 新年好 and pay New Year calls (拜年), closest family first ([People's Daily, 2021](http://sn.people.com.cn/n2/2021/0219/c378302-34581906.html)). In many regions married daughters visit their own parents on the second day (回娘家), but the day differs locally ([People's Daily, 2024](http://sd.people.com.cn/n2/2024/0211/c166192-40745014.html), [Xinhua, 2024](http://www.news.cn/local/20240211/fb5a75a69fca43e7bef4c8853518fe42/c.html)). At Qingming, don't wish 清明节快乐 in connection with grave visits; 清明安康 is the safe wish ([Guangming Online, 2025](https://m.gmw.cn/2025-04/03/content_1304007150.htm), [China News Service, 2025](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/life/2025/04-04/10394679.shtml)). At the Dragon Boat Festival both 端午安康 and 端午节快乐 are correct ([Guangming Online, 2026](https://culture.gmw.cn/2026-06/18/content_38835765.htm)). Mid-Autumn is a family reunion with mooncakes, which are also given to relatives and business partners ([LTL](https://ltl-school.com/mid-autumn-festival/), [HSK 6 sample](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/HSK%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E%EF%BC%89/v3/H61003.pdf)). The tip on New Year taboos is **UNVERIFIED** and hidden.
9. **Compliments and personal questions.** Answering praise with a modest deflection is still the norm, but the everyday forms have changed: 没有没有 or 哪有, or 谢谢，我还在学习 ("thanks, I'm still learning"); 您过奖了 and 还差得远呢 are polite with elders; 哪里哪里 is understood everywhere but sounds bookish and old-fashioned to many younger speakers ([function list, modesty](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%201/%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E/2002-%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E-%E9%AB%98%E7%AD%89%E5%AD%A6%E6%A0%A1%E5%A4%96%E5%9B%BD%E7%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E7%94%9F%E6%B1%89%E8%AF%AD%E6%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E9%95%BF%E6%9C%9F%E8%BF%9B%E4%BF%AE%EF%BC%89-%E9%99%84%E4%BB%B6.pdf#page=237), [现代汉语词典, 过奖](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/master/XDHYCD7th.txt#L19913), [Yoyo Chinese](https://www.yoyochinese.com/blog/learn-mandarin-chinese-how-to-deflect-a-compliment-in-chinese)). Expect questions about your job, age, salary, and when you will marry and have children; a vague, friendly answer (还可以, 以后再说吧) is the usual way out, and your partner can field the hardest ones ([China Daily, 2016](https://language.chinadaily.com.cn/2016-02/03/content_23373952.htm), [China News Service, 2023](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/sh/2023/01-25/9941474.shtml)). 吃了吗？ ("Have you eaten?") is a greeting, heard mostly from older people; answer 吃了，您呢？ ([function list, greetings](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%201/%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E/2002-%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E6%B1%89%E5%8A%9E-%E9%AB%98%E7%AD%89%E5%AD%A6%E6%A0%A1%E5%A4%96%E5%9B%BD%E7%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E7%94%9F%E6%B1%89%E8%AF%AD%E6%95%99%E5%AD%A6%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E9%95%BF%E6%9C%9F%E8%BF%9B%E4%BF%AE%EF%BC%89-%E9%99%84%E4%BB%B6.pdf#page=197), [Language Log](https://languagelog.ldc.upenn.edu/nll/?p=35579)).
10. **Titles at work.** Address people as surname + title or profession (王总, 李经理, 王主任, 王工, 张老师), or surname + 先生/女士; when unsure between two titles, choose the higher one; 老师 is a neutral fallback ([2025 HSK syllabus](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2025/%E6%96%B0%E7%89%88HSK%E8%80%83%E8%AF%95%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E6%97%A0%E6%B0%B4%E5%8D%B0%E7%89%88%EF%BC%89.pdf), [T/ISCLT 002-2023](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/more/TISCLT%20002-2023%20%E8%81%8C%E4%B8%9A%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E8%83%BD%E5%8A%9B%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86.pdf), [BCT A](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTA.pdf), [China News Service, 2017](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/m/sh/2017/08-04/8295738.shtml)). Don't call a superior or client 小X, and keep 老X for peers you know well ([新华 data](https://github.com/pwxcoo/chinese-xinhua/blob/fe6d6c2e8baa82187f4c96bbe042e43f96c05666/data/word.json), [BCT A](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTA.pdf)). Avoid a bare 小姐, which picked up the sense "prostitute"; use 女士 or the title ([CC-CEDICT](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict), [河海大学学报, 2005](https://jour.hhu.edu.cn/hhdxxbsk/article/pdf/sk20050118)). Say 您 to superiors, clients and older colleagues, and avoid 您们 in speech (use 您二位, 各位 or 大家) ([现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt), [BCT A](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%202/%E8%80%83%E7%BA%B2%E5%92%8C%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7/BCT%E6%A0%B7%E5%8D%B7%EF%BC%88%E8%AF%AD%E8%A8%80%E5%90%88%E4%BD%9C%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%EF%BC%89/BCTA.pdf)).
11. **Business cards and WeChat.** Give and receive cards with both hands, read the card before putting it in a holder or on the table, and don't stuff it into a trouser pocket or write on it in front of the giver; in tech circles scanning a WeChat QR code has largely replaced paper cards ([LC Chinese School](https://lcchineseschool.com/?p=1556), [Juwai](https://list.juwai.com/chinese-buyer-tips/2016/06/tip-06-master-chinese-business-card-etiquette), [HSK Lord](https://hsklord.com/topics/meeting-new-people); confidence medium). On WeChat at work, don't send a bare 在吗 ("are you there?") and wait; type rather than send voice messages to superiors and clients (ask 方便语音吗？ first); confirm important messages with 收到; and avoid work messages before 8:00 or after 22:00 ([China News Service, 2022](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/cul/2022/10-20/9876863.shtml), [12371.cn](https://www.12371.cn/2018/09/06/ARTI1536188694435804.shtml), [Jiemian](https://m.jiemian.com/article/8748392.html)). Reply to a notice from your manager with 收到 or 好的, and for a task add what you will do and by when ([36Kr](https://www.36kr.com/p/1724684238849), [Jiemian](https://www.jiemian.com/article/10515163.html)). Avoid the 微笑 smiley: many younger Chinese read it as cold or sarcastic, while many older people mean it sincerely ([CNA](https://www.cna.com.tw/news/acn/201906080084.aspx), [The Paper](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_24067404)).
12. **Indirect no's.** 研究研究 ("we'll look into it"), 过两天再说 and 等研究后再答复你 postpone a decision; read them as "not decided, possibly no". The 现代汉语词典 even uses 研究研究 as its example of a byword for brushing someone off ([现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt)). To disagree, first acknowledge the other view (我理解你的想法), then give yours with 不过 or 只是, then a reason; soften a refusal (恐怕不行, 这个有点难) and offer an alternative ([eChineseLearning](https://www.echineselearning.com/blog/how-to-argue-in-chinese-practical-debate-and-rebuttal-skills), [LTL](https://ltl-school.com/no-in-chinese/)).

**Decision.** Etiquette is taught as tips inside each scene, with their sources, and where customs differ by region or family the tip says so and tells you to ask your partner. **Today** all of the above is in the app except what is marked **UNVERIFIED**; the tips show their sources but not their confidence level ([scene.js](../app/src/views/scene.js)). **Later:** a native speaker, ideally your partner's family, confirms the medium- and low-confidence tips.

### 3.7 Regional notes for a family from south and central China

- **Grandparents on the mother's side** are 外公 and 外婆 in the south; 姥爷 and 姥姥 are the northern pair. Both are HSK level 5 and understood everywhere. The 现代汉语词典 labels 外公/外婆 as regional; when a Shanghai textbook replaced 外婆 with 姥姥 in 2018 on the grounds that 外婆 is a dialect word, it caused a public outcry ([relationship.js](https://github.com/mumuy/relationship/blob/35c44427befb6fd59e16f583b5b92a680a7948db/src/module/data/main.js), [northern variant](https://github.com/mumuy/relationship/blob/35c44427befb6fd59e16f583b5b92a680a7948db/src/locale/north.js), [2025 HSK syllabus](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2025/%E6%96%B0%E7%89%88HSK%E8%80%83%E8%AF%95%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E6%97%A0%E6%B0%B4%E5%8D%B0%E7%89%88%EF%BC%89.pdf), [China News Service, 2018](https://www.chinanews.com/sh/2018/06-23/8544637.shtml)). The app teaches both and says to expect 外公/外婆 (or a local word) in a family from south or central China, and to check with your partner.
- **您.** Forum reports say 您 is far more habitual in the north and that many southerners say 你 even to elders; this is anecdotal ([Chinese-forums](https://www.chinese-forums.com/forums/topic/3575-%E6%82%A8/), [Chinese-forums](https://chinese-forums.com/forums/topic/38639-being-less-formal/page/2)). 您 is never wrong at a first meeting; follow the family's lead after that.
- **请问, not 劳驾,** to stop a stranger in the south (3.1).
- **哪里 rather than 哪儿.** The 儿 ending (erhua) is northern; typed native dialogue has 哪里 687 times against 60 for 哪儿 ([AllSet Learning](https://resources.allsetlearning.com/chinese/pronunciation/Accent), [CrossWOZ](https://github.com/thu-coai/CrossWOZ/blob/master/data/crosswoz/train.json.zip)).
- **Kinship words of the region.** The 现代汉语词典 labels words such as 娭毑 (grandmother) and 爹爹 (either father or grandfather) as regional without naming provinces, and in some families 婆婆 means grandma rather than mother-in-law ([现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt)). The Cantonese variant of relationship.js gives 阿公 and 阿婆 for the mother's parents ([Cantonese variant](https://github.com/mumuy/relationship/blob/35c44427befb6fd59e16f583b5b92a680a7948db/src/locale/guangdong.js)). Ask your partner which words the family uses.
- **New Year food.** Dumplings are a northern must; southern tables have 年糕, fish and regional dishes, and in some southern areas people eat sweet rice balls (汤圆) on New Year's morning ([LTL](https://ltl-school.com/chinese-new-year/), [CCTV, 15 Jan 2023](https://github.com/DuckBurnIncense/xin-wen-lian-bo/blob/31df1d3c9cd2c5fbb766939513968cc8bcf11511/news/20230115.md)). Fish is often left partly uneaten so that there is "surplus" (年年有余), and in Hubei a "look-at fish" (看鱼) is not eaten at all: watch the family before finishing the fish ([CNR](http://country.cnr.cn/gundong/20160208/t20160208_521358674.shtml)).
- **Lantern Festival** sweets are 汤圆 in the south and 元宵 in the north ([China News Service](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/m/cul/2015/03-06/7107713.shtml)).
- **The "little New Year" (小年)** falls on the 24th day of the 12th lunar month in the south, the 23rd in the north: Sunday 31 January 2027 in the south ([现代汉语词典, 小年](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt#L57313), [People's Daily](http://sn.people.com.cn/n2/2021/0219/c378302-34581906.html)).
- **Red-envelope amounts** differ a lot: in Guangdong 5-10 yuan is common and 50 counts as generous, while a 2026 article puts ordinary relatives' children at about 200-500 yuan in Zhejiang or Shanghai and 500-1,000 in parts of Fujian. These are media figures, not statistics ([The Paper and related 2026 articles](https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_26353182)). Ask your partner for the family's usual sum.
- **Spicy food.** 微辣 ("mild") in Sichuan or Chongqing can still be very hot, and in Hunan even a dish ordered without chili may come out spicy; both points are anecdotal, from a forum post reported by The Paper and a Zhihu answer ([The Paper](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_2550399), [Zhihu](https://www.zhihu.com/question/300516959/answer/648463132)).
- **Fireworks** are regulated city by city; in 2026 Zhengzhou, for example, allowed them only outside the 4th Ring Road at set hours ([Zhengzhou government](https://public.zhengzhou.gov.cn/D0102X/9884010.jhtml)).
- **Dutch consular help.** The consulate-general in Guangzhou covers Guangdong, Guangxi, Fujian and Hainan, the one in Shanghai covers Shanghai, Jiangsu, Zhejiang and Anhui, and the embassy in Beijing covers all other provinces, including Hunan, Hubei, Jiangxi, Henan, Sichuan, Chongqing, Guizhou and Yunnan. The ministry's 24/7 line is +31 247 247 247 ([NederlandWereldwijd open data](https://github.com/NWW-team/Reisadvies-Reviewer/blob/253b9b3ae4d6a021e1ba5d00a8ce34a23c3ec049/data/posten/CHN.xml), [NetherlandsWorldwide](https://www.netherlandsworldwide.nl/contact/embassies-consulates-general/hong-kong/embassy-beijing)). Confidence medium.

**Decision.** The app teaches standard Mandarin (普通话) as the norm and gives regional words and customs as notes, without guessing the province: the research could not pin most regional words to provinces. **Today** the notes appear on phrase cards (*Regional*), in the tips and on the kinship chart ([scene.js](../app/src/views/scene.js), [kinship.js](../app/src/views/kinship.js)). **Later:** once you know the family's own words and habits, they can be added as notes for that province.

### 3.8 Practice dialogues

[dialogues.json](../data/generated/dialogues.json) holds 36 short dialogues: 16 for travel, 10 for family and 10 for work, 179 lines in all, 4 to 8 lines each. Most scenes have two; *Trains* and *Allergies* have one. Five scenes have none because their verified phrases do not make a natural two-sided exchange: *Pharmacy, doctor and emergencies*, *Red envelopes*, *Other festivals*, *Titles and forms of address* and *WeChat and email*.

How they were made: an AI agent arranged verified phrases of one scene into a conversation and wrote no Chinese of its own. A second agent checked each dialogue line by line. It left 29 unchanged, fixed the notes of 2, and changed 5: for example, it swapped two lines in *You spoke too fast*, because 没问题，有不明白的问我就行 ("no problem, just ask me") answers a request, not "you spoke too fast", and in *Taxi to the train station* it gave the line about turning right at the junction to the driver instead of you. The validator checks that every line is a phrase of the same scene and that the speaker matches who may say it (*you*, *them* or *either*); the build drops a dialogue if any of its lines is not verified ([validate.mjs](../scripts/validate.mjs), [content.mjs](../scripts/lib/content.mjs)). All 36 are in the app.

In the app each dialogue says "Practice dialogue assembled by AI from this scene's checked phrases (not yet reviewed by a teacher). Every line is a verified phrase.", and a listening mode hides the other speaker's text ([dialogue.js](../app/src/views/dialogue.js)). All 36 have `reviewed: false` and are written out in full in [AI-CONTENT.md](AI-CONTENT.md).

**Decision.** Dialogues may only arrange verified phrases of their own scene, so the Chinese in them is as reliable as the phrases; whether they flow naturally is an AI judgement. **Later:** your partner or a teacher should read them in [AI-CONTENT.md](AI-CONTENT.md) and set `reviewed: true` on each one that sounds natural.

### 3.9 Pitfalls and grammar

All 24 pitfalls and all 18 grammar points are verified and in the app, with 96 and 111 examples. Every example has its own source; 12 pitfall examples and 7 grammar examples are wrong sentences that show the mistake, marked with a cross and never played aloud ([reference.js](../app/src/views/reference.js)). The grammar points and most pitfalls follow the national standard GF 0025-2021 and the 2025 syllabus; real learner sentences from the corpora YACLC and MuCGEC show the mistakes in 12 pitfalls and 9 grammar points; the sound pitfalls use the 现代汉语词典, IPA data from WikiPron and, for Dutch speakers, a 1982 Dutch article on transcribing Chinese. The verifiers confirmed 15 pitfalls and corrected 9, and confirmed 8 grammar points and corrected 10.

**Pitfalls** (9 on pronunciation and pinyin, 15 on grammar and word choice):

| Pitfall | Rule in one line | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Third-tone sandhi (你好 is said ní hǎo) and the half-third tone | A 3rd tone before another 3rd tone is said as a 2nd tone (你好 is said ní hǎo); before other tones it is a low "half-third" without the final rise. | [现代汉语词典, front matter](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/master/%E6%AD%A3%E6%96%87%E4%BB%A5%E5%A4%96%E9%83%A8%E5%88%86.txt), [PaddleSpeech tone sandhi](https://github.com/PaddlePaddle/PaddleSpeech/blob/develop/paddlespeech/t2s/frontend/tone_sandhi.py) |
| 不 bù becomes bú before a 4th tone, and is light in 好不好 / 听不懂 | 不 is bù, but bú before a 4th tone (不是 bú shì); it is light in 好不好 and 听不懂. | [2025 HSK syllabus](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2025/%E6%96%B0%E7%89%88HSK%E8%80%83%E8%AF%95%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E6%97%A0%E6%B0%B4%E5%8D%B0%E7%89%88%EF%BC%89.pdf), [WikiPron Mandarin IPA](https://github.com/CUNY-CL/wikipron/blob/master/data/scrape/tsv/cmn_hani_standard_broad.tsv) |
| 一 yī: yí before a 4th tone, yì before other tones, yī as a number | 一 is yī alone, when counting, as an ordinal and in dates; yí before a 4th tone (一个 yí ge), yì before the other tones (一天 yìtiān). | [2025 HSK syllabus](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2025/%E6%96%B0%E7%89%88HSK%E8%80%83%E8%AF%95%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E6%97%A0%E6%B0%B4%E5%8D%B0%E7%89%88%EF%BC%89.pdf), [WikiPron Mandarin IPA](https://github.com/CUNY-CL/wikipron/blob/master/data/scrape/tsv/cmn_hani_standard_broad.tsv) |
| ü: written u after j, q, x, y — but nǚ ≠ nǔ and lǜ ≠ lù | ü keeps its dots after n and l (女 nǚ is not 努 nǔ); after j, q, x and y a written u is the ü sound (去 qù, 鱼 yú). | [pypinyin (quoting the 1958 pinyin scheme)](https://github.com/mozillazg/python-pinyin/blob/master/docs/usage.rst), [现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/master/XDHYCD7th.txt) |
| zh ch sh r vs z c s vs j q x (and the 'i' in shi / si) | zh ch sh r (tongue tip raised), z c s (at the teeth) and j q x (tongue body at the palate) are three different series. | [现代汉语词典, front matter](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/master/%E6%AD%A3%E6%96%87%E4%BB%A5%E5%A4%96%E9%83%A8%E5%88%86.txt), [WikiPron Mandarin IPA](https://github.com/CUNY-CL/wikipron/blob/master/data/scrape/tsv/cmn_hani_standard_broad.tsv) |
| Vowels that don't sound like they look: e, ian, ui, iu, un, ong | e alone is a back "uh" (喝 hē); ian sounds like "yen"; iu, ui and un hide a vowel (iou, uei, uen). | [pypinyin (quoting the 1958 pinyin scheme)](https://github.com/mozillazg/python-pinyin/blob/master/docs/usage.rst), [WikiPron Mandarin IPA](https://github.com/CUNY-CL/wikipron/blob/master/data/scrape/tsv/cmn_hani_standard_broad.tsv) |
| b/p, d/t, g/k: the difference is a puff of air, not voicing | b/p, d/t, g/k (and z/c, zh/ch, j/q) differ by a puff of air, not by voicing. | [现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/master/XDHYCD7th.txt), [WikiPron Mandarin IPA](https://github.com/CUNY-CL/wikipron/blob/master/data/scrape/tsv/cmn_hani_standard_broad.tsv) |
| Dutch spelling habits that mislead in pinyin (u/oe/uu, j, g, h, w, z, ui, ie, ou) | Read pinyin letters with pinyin values, not Dutch ones: u is Dutch oe (uu only after j, q, x, y), h is the Dutch ch, j is never the Dutch j, g is never the Dutch g. | [Yang 1982 (DBNL)](https://www.dbnl.org/tekst/_yan001198201_01/_yan001198201_01_0080.php), [WikiPron Dutch IPA](https://github.com/CUNY-CL/wikipron/blob/master/data/scrape/tsv/nld_latn_broad.tsv) |
| Neutral tone: particles and toneless second syllables (东西 dōngxi vs dōngxī) | Particles (的, 了, 吗, 呢, 吧) and some second syllables are toneless; 东西 dōngxi "thing" is not dōngxī "east and west". | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/master/XDHYCD7th.txt) |
| 了 is not a past-tense marker | 了 marks a completed action or a new situation, not the past tense; a past event is negated with 没 and no 了. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/main/valid.jsonl) |
| 不 vs 没(有): 'not' vs 'didn't / doesn't have' | 不 negates states, habits and plans; 没(有) negates what did not happen; 有 is only negated with 没. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/main/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |
| Number + measure word + noun (一个人, 两本书) | A number needs a measure word before the noun (两本书); 个 is the general one. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/main/valid.jsonl) |
| 的, 地, 得: three particles, one sound (de) | 的 goes before a noun, 地 before a verb, 得 after a verb or adjective; all three are said de. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/main/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |
| 二 èr vs 两 liǎng | Use 两 before a measure word and for 2 o'clock (两个, 两点); use 二 when counting, inside numbers (十二) and when reading out digits. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/main/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |
| 会, 能, 可以: three words for 'can' | 会 = a learned skill or a likelihood, 能 = able to or allowed by circumstances, 可以 = permitted. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/main/valid.jsonl) |
| 您们 is not the polite plural: use 大家, 各位 or 你们 | 您们 is not standard in speech: say 你们, 大家, 各位, or 您二位 / 您俩. | [2025 HSK syllabus](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2025/%E6%96%B0%E7%89%88HSK%E8%80%83%E8%AF%95%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E6%97%A0%E6%B0%B4%E5%8D%B0%E7%89%88%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/main/valid.jsonl) |
| Replying to 谢谢: 不客气, 不用谢, 没事 — 没关系 answers an apology | Answer thanks with 不客气 or 不用谢; 没关系 answers an apology. | [2025 HSK syllabus](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2025/%E6%96%B0%E7%89%88HSK%E8%80%83%E8%AF%95%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E6%97%A0%E6%B0%B4%E5%8D%B0%E7%89%88%EF%BC%89.pdf), [CC-CEDICT](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict) |
| 对不起 vs 不好意思 vs 抱歉; 打扰一下 / 请问 for 'excuse me' | 对不起 is a real apology, 不好意思 a lighter "sorry" and the everyday "excuse me", 抱歉 a "sorry" often heard from staff; 打扰一下 / 请问 get attention. | [2025 HSK syllabus](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2025/%E6%96%B0%E7%89%88HSK%E8%80%83%E8%AF%95%E5%A4%A7%E7%BA%B2%EF%BC%88%E6%97%A0%E6%B0%B4%E5%8D%B0%E7%89%88%EF%BC%89.pdf), [CC-CEDICT](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict) |
| No 是 before adjectives: 我很忙, not *我是忙 | No 是 before an adjective: 我很忙, not 我是忙. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/main/valid.jsonl) |
| 比 comparisons take no 很 | No 很 in a 比 comparison: 他比我高; use 更 or 还, or put the amount after the adjective. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/main/valid.jsonl) |
| Question words stay in place, and a what/where/who question takes no 吗 | A question word stays where the answer goes (你买什么?); no inversion, and no 吗 on a what/where/who question. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [CC-CEDICT](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict) |
| Time and place go before the verb | Time and place come before the verb: 我每天在家学习. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/main/valid.jsonl) |
| 和 joins nouns and short phrases, not sentences | 和 joins nouns and short phrases, not two sentences. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/main/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |
| 也 and 都 go after the subject, right before the verb | 也 and 都 stand after the subject and before the verb: 他也是学生. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/main/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/main/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |

**Grammar points** (HSK 1-3):

| Point | Rule in one line | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Basic word order: subject + time + place + verb + object | Subject + time + place + verb + object: 哥哥在北京学中文. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/bf17c54e8f55d36b019bef782eccbb1cdd027e81/valid.jsonl) |
| 是 links two nouns: A 是 B | 是 links two nouns (他是我的老师); the negative is 不是. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt) |
| Adjective predicates: subject + 很 + adjective (no 是) | Subject + 很 + adjective, without 是 (我很忙); 很 here hardly means "very". | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/bf17c54e8f55d36b019bef782eccbb1cdd027e81/valid.jsonl) |
| 有 / 没有 (to have, there is) and 在 (to be located) | 有 = have / there is (negative 没有, never 不有); person or thing + 在 + place = is at. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt) |
| Negation: 不 vs 没(有), and 别 | 不 = not / won't, 没(有) = didn't / haven't, 别 = don't! | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/39221934ee4ccf07801879136a75dd7e11319977/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |
| Yes/no questions: 吗 and the affirmative-negative (A-not-A) form | Yes/no questions: statement + 吗, or the A-not-A form (你忙不忙?). | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt) |
| Question words: 什么, 谁, 哪儿/哪里, 哪, 几, 多少, 怎么, 为什么, 什么时候, 怎么样 | 什么, 谁, 哪儿 / 哪里, 几, 多少, 怎么… stand where the answer goes; 哪里 is the usual form in the south and in writing. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [AllSet Learning (north and south)](https://resources.allsetlearning.com/chinese/pronunciation/Accent) |
| 呢: 'And you?', 'Where is…?' and follow-up questions | Noun + 呢? asks "and …?" (我去医院，你呢?) or "where is …?" (笔呢?). | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [CC-CEDICT](https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page=cc-cedict) |
| Measure words: number / 这 / 那 / 哪 / 几 + measure word + noun | Number / 这 / 那 / 哪 / 几 + measure word + noun; "two" before a measure word is 两. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/39221934ee4ccf07801879136a75dd7e11319977/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |
| 的, 得, 地: the three 'de' particles | 的 before a noun, 得 after a verb for how well (说得很好), 地 before a verb for how. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [YACLC learner corpus](https://github.com/blcuicall/YACLC/blob/bf17c54e8f55d36b019bef782eccbb1cdd027e81/valid.jsonl) |
| 了: completed action (verb + 了) and change of situation (sentence + 了) | Verb + 了 = completed; sentence + 了 = new situation; 了 is not a past tense. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/39221934ee4ccf07801879136a75dd7e11319977/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |
| 过: past experience ('have done / have ever done') | Verb + 过 = have done at least once (我去过中国); negative 没 + verb + 过. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/39221934ee4ccf07801879136a75dd7e11319977/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |
| 在 / 正在 + verb (… 呢): actions in progress | 在 / 正在 + verb (+ 呢) = doing it right now. | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [现代汉语词典](https://github.com/CNMan/XDHYCD7th/blob/1e38ce15528b2fc3fdee0b2934b0951fcac51cb9/XDHYCD7th.txt) |
| Modal verbs: 想, 要, 会, 能, 可以 | 想 would like, 要 want / will / need, 会 know how / will, 能 be able, 可以 may; 不要 + verb means "don't". | [GF 0025-2021 grammar](https://github.com/docrealm/docs-hsk/blob/bdff23362be0ffb4020505b7333a4443bcaa9ec1/HSK%203/HSK%203-2021/2021-%E5%9B%BD%E9%99%85%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%95%99%E8%82%B2%E4%B8%AD%E6%96%87%E6%B0%B4%E5%B9%B3%E7%AD%89%E7%BA%A7%E6%A0%87%E5%87%86%EF%BC%88%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E5%88%86%E5%86%8C%20%E8%AF%AD%E6%B3%95%EF%BC%89.pdf), [MuCGEC learner corpus](https://github.com/HillZhang1999/MuCGEC/blob/39221934ee4ccf07801879136a75dd7e11319977/data/MuCGEC/MuCGEC_dev.txt) |
