A pitch tracker turns a recording into a line that can be drawn next to a native speaker's line. It needs only the microphone and some arithmetic, so it can run on the phone, offline.

**It works on both phones (verified).**

| What is needed | iPhone | Android Chrome |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Microphone access (`getUserMedia`, HTTPS only) | iOS 11+; inside Home Screen apps from iOS 13.4, not before ([caniuse](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Fyrd/caniuse/main/features-json/stream.json), [MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz)) | Chrome 53+ ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz)) |
| Recording to a file (`MediaRecorder`) | iOS 14.5+ ([caniuse](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Fyrd/caniuse/main/features-json/mediarecorder.json)) | Chrome 47+ ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz)) |
| Raw samples processed off the main thread (`AudioWorklet`) | iOS 14.5+ ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz)) | Chrome 66+ ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz)) |
| Starting audio | only from a tap: a new audio context stays paused until a user action resumes it ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz)) | – |
| Switching off the phone's voice processing | echo cancellation only; switching it off makes Safari record through Apple's plain audio unit instead of its voice-processing unit ([WebKit source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WebKit/WebKit/WebKit-7625.2.8/Source/WebCore/platform/mediastream/cocoa/CoreAudioCaptureUnit.cpp)) | echo cancellation (Chrome 59+), noise suppression and automatic gain (Chrome 67+) ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz)) |

The sources differ slightly on `MediaRecorder` for iPhones: MDN says iOS 14, caniuse says 14.5 (14.0 to 14.4 needed an experimental setting) ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz), [caniuse](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Fyrd/caniuse/main/features-json/mediarecorder.json)). The table uses caniuse's later version; for current iPhones the difference does not matter.

The research suggests recording with all three kinds of voice processing switched off, since playback and recording happen one after the other and no echo cancellation is needed, and reading back which settings the browser actually applied ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz), [WebKit source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WebKit/WebKit/WebKit-7625.2.8/Source/WebCore/platform/mediastream/cocoa/CoreAudioCaptureUnit.cpp)). How much the default processing bends a pitch line has not been measured: **UNVERIFIED**. Whether an iPhone Home Screen app remembers the microphone permission between launches is also **UNVERIFIED**; the browser always needs the user's permission, and a refusal arrives as a `NotAllowedError` that the app must explain ([MDN getUserMedia](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/content/main/files/en-us/web/api/mediadevices/getusermedia/index.md)).

**Methods and libraries.** The usual pitch trackers for speech are YIN (de Cheveigné and Kawahara, 2002) ([aubio](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/aubio/aubio/master/README.md)), the McLeod Pitch Method used by the pitchy library ([pitchy](https://registry.npmjs.org/pitchy/-/pitchy-4.1.0.tgz)), and Praat's filtered autocorrelation, which Praat has recommended for intonation since 2023 ([Praat manual](https://github.com/praat/praat.git)). Neural trackers such as CREPE (MIT licence) exist for offline work ([PyPI](https://pypi.org/pypi/crepe/json)).

| Library | Licence | Size | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| pitchy 4.1.0 (2024-01-04) | MIT ([pitchy](https://registry.npmjs.org/pitchy/-/pitchy-4.1.0.tgz)) | 5.4 kB gzipped, plus fft.js 4.0.4 (MIT, 2.8 kB gzipped) ([fft.js](https://registry.npmjs.org/fft.js)) | best fit if a library is used |
| pitchfinder 2.3.4 (2025-12-16) | only "GNU v3" in its package file and no licence text: copyleft in every reading ([pitchfinder](https://registry.npmjs.org/pitchfinder/-/pitchfinder-2.3.4.tgz)) | 228,607 bytes unpacked | avoid |
| aubiojs 0.2.1 (2022-11-08) | its own licence file is MIT-style, but it bundles aubio, which is GPL-3.0-or-later ([aubiojs](https://registry.npmjs.org/aubiojs/-/aubiojs-0.2.1.tgz), [aubio](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/aubio/aubio/master/README.md)) | 70 kB gzipped | avoid |
| own YIN or McLeod code | – | – | the research's alternative: no dependency |

These licence notes are an engineering summary, not legal advice.

**What goes wrong.** Praat's manual describes the traps that matter for Mandarin ([Praat manual](https://github.com/praat/praat.git)):

- Tone 3 can turn creaky at its lowest point, and creaky voice can go as low as 30 Hz, below a tracker's floor. The manual gives "the Chinese speaker is in the middle of a third tone" as an example. A lower floor helps but needs a longer analysis window (60 ms at a 50 Hz floor, 120 ms at 25 Hz), which blurs fast changes.
- The end of a fourth tone can fall too fast to follow.
- Octave errors: background noise can halve the measured pitch (200 Hz read as 100 Hz), and a strong first formant can make 150 Hz read as 450 Hz. pitchfinder's documentation also warns that YIN "occasionally provides values that are wildly incorrect" ([pitchfinder](https://registry.npmjs.org/pitchfinder/-/pitchfinder-2.3.4.tgz)).
- The search range must suit the voice; Praat's standard range is 50 to 800 Hz.

The research concludes: show the shapes and do not score them; leave gaps where the voice is unvoiced or creaky instead of drawing through them; and never mark a third tone wrong because its low part is missing.

**Comparing two voices.** "Speakers can vary enormously in their pitch ranges", as Praat's manual puts it ([Praat manual](https://github.com/praat/praat.git)), so the raw hertz of a native speaker and of the learner cannot be compared. Each line is converted to semitones relative to that speaker's own middle (median) pitch, the way Praat converts units: semitones = 12 × log2(f / reference) ([Praat source](https://github.com/praat/praat.git)), and both lines are stretched to the same length. Using the median as the reference is a design choice; no study comparing alternatives could be checked.

**Native reference lines.** The research recommends computing them once, offline, with Praat (for example through praat-parselmouth 0.4.7, GPL-3.0-or-later, used only as a build tool) and shipping the numbers next to the audio, so that a person can spot-check them ([PyPI](https://pypi.org/pypi/praat-parselmouth/json), [Praat manual](https://github.com/praat/praat.git)).

**Server headers.** By default a site may use the microphone on its own pages (the default allowlist is `self`), so no header is needed, and a `Permissions-Policy` header must never say `microphone=()`, which makes Chrome refuse with `NotAllowedError` ([MDN Permissions-Policy](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/content/main/files/en-us/web/http/reference/headers/permissions-policy/microphone/index.md)); Safari ignores this header altogether ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz)). In the Content-Security-Policy, playing a recording from a `blob:` address needs `media-src 'self' blob:`, an audio worklet counts as a script (`script-src`), and any cloud connection falls under `connect-src` ([CSP3](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/w3c/webappsec-csp/main/index.bs), [Fetch standard](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/whatwg/fetch/main/fetch.bs)).

**Does seeing pitch lines help? Only thin evidence.** The speech research could not check any study (web search was unavailable and academic sites were blocked). The learning-method research (section 4) found small studies only: a real-time pitch display next to the model's improved French learners' prosody ([Hardison 2004](https://www.lltjournal.org/item/312/)), and a small Mandarin pilot of audio-visual training found better-rated tones ([Wang 2012](https://www.igi-global.com/article/auditory-visual-training-mandarin-tones/67123/)). No controlled study of Mandarin learners seeing their own pitch line was found, so whether it helps is **UNVERIFIED**, and the pitch line is an experiment, not a proven teaching method.

**Decision:** pitch tracking works on both phones, works offline and keeps the voice on the phone, so v1 includes it as a Beta in the tone trainer, showing shapes only and no score. What was built is in 5.4; what waits is in 5.5.

### 5.4 What the app does today

**Self-grading in a study session.** A Speak card shows the English meaning. You say the Chinese out loud, tap *Show answer* to see the characters and pinyin and hear the model, and grade yourself *Again*, *Hard*, *Good* or *Easy*; the FSRS scheduler uses that grade to set the next review (section 4) ([study.js](../app/src/views/study.js)). An optional *Record yourself* button records you before you check (up to 8 seconds); after *Show answer* you can play *Mine* or *Mine, then the model*. The recording stays only in the phone's memory ([recorder.js](../app/src/recorder.js)), and the card draws no pitch line.

**Record and compare (Beta).** Open *Tones → Record and compare*, or tap *Record* on a syllable in the pinyin chart ([tones.js](../app/src/views/tones.js)):

1. Choose what to say: a syllable with tone 1 to 4 (412 syllables × 4 tones, recorded by Chen Wang for audio-cmn; see the [README](../README.md#audio)), typed as `ma3`, `mǎ` or `lv4` or picked at random; or a two-syllable word by tone pattern (19 patterns, up to 24 words each, easiest 2025 HSK level first, only words whose recording keeps the dictionary tones, so there is no tone change to confuse you) ([tones.json](../app/data/tones.json)). Every sound here is a native recording; the device voice is never used, because it may get tones wrong.
2. Play the native recording.
3. Tap *Record*, say it once and tap *Stop* (at most 4 seconds; recording also stops when the app goes to the background). Then *Play yours*, or *Play both*: the native recording, a 0.25-second pause, then yours.
4. The chart shows both pitch lines, the native speaker solid and you dashed, in semitones above or below each speaker's own middle pitch, from the start to the end of the voiced sound. A text line under the chart gives the start, lowest, highest and end values, and screen readers get the same text.
5. The page says plainly that the lines are experimental and can be wrong, that a third tone often breaks off at its creaky low point, and that there is no score.

Your recording stays in the phone's memory and is deleted when you leave the page; nothing is uploaded or stored ([recorder.js](../app/src/recorder.js)). The page and its pitch code belong to the offline app shell, and a native recording works offline once it has been loaded with a connection ([sw.js](../app/sw.js)).

**How the pitch line is made** ([pitch.js](../app/src/lib/pitch.js)):

| Step | In the app | Research basis |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Tracker | the app's own YIN code, no library | own YIN or McLeod code was the research's alternative to pitchy ([pitchy](https://registry.npmjs.org/pitchy/-/pitchy-4.1.0.tgz)) |
| Analysis | audio reduced to about 16 kHz; one pitch value every 10 ms from a 25 ms window; search range 60 to 500 Hz | Praat's standard range is 50 to 800 Hz, and creak can reach 30 Hz, so creak below 60 Hz shows as a gap ([Praat manual](https://github.com/praat/praat.git)) |
| What counts as voice | clearly periodic and loud enough (within 35 dB of the loudest moment); weaker frames only where they continue a voiced neighbour within 3 semitones | gate frames on clarity and volume ([pitchy](https://registry.npmjs.org/pitchy/-/pitchy-4.1.0.tgz)) |
| Cleaning | voiced pieces shorter than 30 ms dropped, octave jumps folded back, 5-frame median filter | octave errors ([Praat manual](https://github.com/praat/praat.git)) |
| Gaps | gaps up to 60 ms bridged, longer ones left as breaks; less than 60 ms of voiced sound gives no line | show gaps instead of joining through unvoiced or creaky parts ([Praat manual](https://github.com/praat/praat.git)) |
| Scale | semitones relative to the speaker's median pitch; 64 points from start to end | semitone conversion as in Praat ([Praat source](https://github.com/praat/praat.git)) |

The 30 automated tests with synthetic sounds pass ([pitch.test.mjs](../app/tests/pitch.test.mjs), run on 2026-11-02). They cover pure tones (within 0.3 %), a missing fundamental, strong overtones without octave errors, a rising 150 → 300 Hz glide and a falling 280 → 140 Hz glide like a fourth tone, silence (never counted as voice) and noise (hardly ever), octave errors folded back while real glides stay, and the same shape an octave higher giving the same semitone line. No test uses real voices or phones, so nothing has measured how accurate the line is for a learner's third and fourth tones.

**Microphone and headers.** The app asks for the microphone only inside a *Record* or *Record yourself* tap, with echo cancellation and noise suppression switched off ([recorder.js](../app/src/recorder.js)). [app/vercel.json](../app/vercel.json) sends `Permissions-Policy: camera=(), geolocation=(), microphone=(self)` and a Content-Security-Policy with `media-src 'self' blob:`, `script-src 'self'` and `connect-src 'self'`, as recommended above. The app records with `MediaRecorder` rather than an audio worklet and uses no WebAssembly, so no further policy entries are needed.

**Where the build differs from the research plan**

- The native lines are computed on the phone by the same tracker, not offline with Praat, so they can contain the same tracking errors as yours.
- Automatic gain control is left on; the research suggested switching it off. On an iPhone this makes no difference, because only echo cancellation can be switched there ([MDN compat data](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz)); on Android it applies. The app does not read back which settings the browser applied.
- *Play both* plays once; there is no loop.
- The record page has no self-rating and does not feed the scheduler. Speak cards in a study session can record you, but they play your attempt before the model and draw no pitch line. Self-grading uses FSRS's four grades rather than the three-level rating the research sketched.

The listening drills in the same tone trainer (single syllables, two-syllable words, minimal pairs) are covered in section 4.

### 5.5 What waits, and why

| Waits | Why | What would settle it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A speech-recognition check ("the recogniser heard …") | no tones; documented as unavailable in the iPhone Home Screen app; audio may go to Apple or Google; no usable confidence on Android (5.1) | a test on an iPhone (Safari tab and Home Screen app, iOS 26/27) and an Android phone with HSK 1-2 words and minimal pairs; at most an optional extra in a browser tab afterwards |
| Cloud scoring with Azure | voice upload needs a GDPR review; a server function and key; price unconfirmed; no tone score (5.2) | a v2 test with deliberate wrong-tone minimal pairs; the EU-region price; the GDPR review |
| Tencent SOE | the only explicit tone scorer, but apparently for mainland-China accounts only (5.2) | Tencent confirming EU access, price and data location |
| Recognition inside the page (sherpa-onnx, transformers.js) | large model downloads, `'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, and text rather than tones (5.1) | – |
| An automatic tone score from the pitch line | creaky third tones, fast fourth tones and octave errors make automatic judgements unreliable (5.3) | a labelled set of real phone recordings and a measured accuracy |
| Native pitch lines computed offline with Praat | not built yet (5.4) | a build step with praat-parselmouth |
| Voice processing fully off, with the applied settings read back | not built yet (5.4) | recordings with processing on and off, compared on both phones |
| Pitch lines inside Speak cards | Speak cards record you and play your attempt, then the model, and their grade feeds the scheduler, but they draw no pitch line (5.4) | – |
| Evidence that seeing their own pitch line helps adult learners of Mandarin | only small studies, none a controlled Mandarin study of this (5.3) | a literature search |
| The microphone permission in the iPhone Home Screen app | not known whether it is remembered between launches (5.3) | a test on an iPhone |

## 6. Hosting, offline and the framework

Where does the app live, what does that cost, and how does it keep working on a phone without a network? This section covers the hosting plan (Vercel's free Hobby plan), the database behind the optional sign-in and sync, how the app works offline and what iPhones and Android phones allow, why the app has no framework and no build step, and how it gets deployed. Everything was checked on 2026-11-02. The choice of FSRS version is in section 4, the recordings themselves in section 2 and the microphone in section 5.

The research produced 30 findings. An independent second pass confirmed 18, corrected 2 and could not re-check 10. Final status: 28 verified, 1 disputed and 1 unverified; both are marked **UNVERIFIED** below. Nine of the ten that could not be re-checked are Vercel's plan figures and terms: web search had run out and vercel.com is blocked from the sandbox the research ran in. Those figures rest on the researcher's reading of Vercel's documentation during the research, and the parts that also appear in Vercel's own command-line tool match it ([Vercel CLI 65.9.0 code](https://registry.npmjs.org/vercel/-/vercel-65.9.0.tgz)). Apart from the disputed file limit (6.2), they stay "verified" at medium confidence. The app's estimated use is far below these figures, so small differences would not change a decision.

**In short.**

| | Decision | In the app today |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Hosting | Vercel Hobby, free. It is allowed for "non-commercial personal use" ([Vercel Fair Use Guidelines](https://vercel.com/docs/limits/fair-use-guidelines)), and one learner's requests and downloads stay under 1% of its monthly allowances (6.1). | Ready to import. The sandbox cannot deploy it, so the learner imports it once (6.7). |
| The 7,359 recordings | Plain files inside the deployment: 7,441 files and 47.3 MB in total, below even the strictest documented limits of 15,000 files and 100 MB ([Vercel Limits](https://vercel.com/docs/limits)) (6.2). | Built. |
| Sync database | Upstash Redis from the Vercel Marketplace; Vercel KV and Vercel Postgres no longer exist ([Redis on Vercel](https://vercel.com/docs/redis), [Postgres on Vercel](https://vercel.com/docs/postgres)) (6.3). | Built, optional. Without it the app works on the device alone. |
| Offline | A hand-written service worker (6.4). | Built: after one online visit the app opens and works without a network. |
| iPhone | Use the app from the Home Screen. Safari deletes a website's data after 7 days of Safari use without any interaction with it, but a Home Screen app's own data is exempt ([WebKit source](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/ee10f6908b65c932143827e6e5dc5992733bfb2c/Source/WebKit/NetworkProcess/Classifier/ResourceLoadStatisticsStore.cpp)) (6.5). | Instructions in *Settings → Offline*. |
| Background sync | None: Safari has no Background Sync ([MDN compat data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/0efedff8c8b6f131ad029443056bc74e00b1aca2/api/SyncManager.json)) (6.5). | Syncs only while the app is open. |
| Framework | None, and no build step: the one library, ts-fsrs 5.4.2, is a single self-contained file ([ts-fsrs 5.4.2 package](https://registry.npmjs.org/ts-fsrs/-/ts-fsrs-5.4.2.tgz)) (6.6). | Plain HTML, CSS and ES modules; ts-fsrs copied in with its code unchanged. |
| AI at runtime | None (6.8). | The app calls no AI service. |

### 6.1 Vercel Hobby: terms, allowances, and what happens at a limit

**Terms.** "Hobby teams are restricted to non-commercial personal use only. All commercial usage of the platform requires either a Pro or Enterprise plan." Commercial means taking payments from visitors, advertising a product or service, showing ads or affiliate links, or being paid to build or host the site; asking for donations is not commercial ([Vercel Fair Use Guidelines](https://vercel.com/docs/limits/fair-use-guidelines)). Vercel's terms also allow it to change or end the Hobby plan at any time ([Vercel Terms of Service](https://vercel.com/legal/terms)). A personal learning app for one learner and the family, with no ads and no payments, fits. If it were ever offered to other learners for money, it would have to move to Pro first.

**Monthly allowances.** Hobby is free ([Vercel Hobby plan](https://vercel.com/docs/plans/hobby), [Vercel pricing](https://vercel.com/pricing)). Every request to the site counts as a CDN request (formerly called an Edge Request), whether Vercel's cache answers it or not ([Vercel CDN usage](https://vercel.com/docs/manage-cdn-usage)). A recording the service worker plays from the phone's own cache never reaches Vercel, because cache first means the network is not asked ([MDN caching guide](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/progressive_web_apps/guides/caching/index.md)).

| Allowance | Hobby, per month ([Vercel Hobby plan](https://vercel.com/docs/plans/hobby)) | This app, one learner (arithmetic) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CDN requests | 1,000,000 | saving all 7,359 recordings once on one phone: 7,359 requests, 0.7% |
| Fast Data Transfer | 100 GB | the whole site once: 47.3 MB, under 0.05% |
| Fast Origin Transfer | 10 GB | – |
| Function invocations | 1,000,000 | one sync per answered card (6.3): about 260 a day after a year at the default pace, 7,800 a month, 0.8% |
| Active CPU / provisioned memory | 4 CPU-hours / 360 GB-hours | not measured |

Sources disagree on one figure. A page for Vercel's older, now "legacy" function pricing lists 100,000 included invocations; the current Hobby page, the pricing page and the page for the current ("Fluid compute") functions all say 1,000,000 ([legacy pricing](https://vercel.com/docs/functions/usage-and-pricing/legacy-pricing), [Fluid compute pricing](https://vercel.com/docs/functions/usage-and-pricing)). This section uses 1,000,000, because three current pages agree and the other is marked legacy.

**Functions.** On Hobby a function runs for at most 300 seconds with 2 GB of memory and 1 vCPU; the request body and the response body may each be 4.5 MB at most; the code bundle may be 250 MB ([Vercel Functions limits](https://vercel.com/docs/functions/limitations)). The app's sync endpoint accepts at most 1 MiB per request and answers with at most about 2 MB of card states, asking the device to fetch the rest in a further request ([http.js](../app/api/_lib/http.js), [sync.js](../app/api/sync.js)), so it stays under the 4.5 MB limit.

**Deployments.** Hobby allows 100 deployments a day. Deployments started by a Git push and by the command-line tool share this quota, and preview deployments count ([Vercel Limits](https://vercel.com/docs/limits), [Vercel Community](https://community.vercel.com/t/being-rate-limited-on-hobby-for-deployment-limits-but-im-well-under-100/49793)). A build may run for 45 minutes, one at a time ([Vercel Limits](https://vercel.com/docs/limits)). This app has no build, and every push to GitHub becomes one deployment, so changes are pushed in batches.

**At a limit.** Hobby users are not billed for extra use ([Vercel Blob pricing](https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-blob/usage-and-pricing)). Instead, Vercel's Hobby page says: "In most cases, if you exceed your usage limits on the Hobby plan, you will have to wait until 30 days have passed before you can use the feature again" ([Vercel Hobby plan](https://vercel.com/docs/plans/hobby)). Warnings come by email and on the dashboard when a team approaches a limit and when it passes one ([Vercel plans](https://vercel.com/docs/plans)). Users report Hobby teams that stayed paused after their usage had dropped, and limits used up by AI crawlers ([Vercel Community](https://community.vercel.com/t/hobby-team-still-paused-after-one-time-unblock-was-used-and-usage-returned-within-limits/51328), [Vercel Community](https://community.vercel.com/t/hobby-plan-usage-limits-exceeded-due-to-ai-bot-crawling/41718)); these are user reports, not statements by Vercel. The risk is a pause, not a bill: a phone that has not saved the app could not load it until the pause ends.

**Decision: host on Hobby.** The terms fit, one learner's estimated use is a small fraction of the allowances, and the worst case at a limit is a pause, never a charge. The app avoids sudden usage: it never polls the server, failed syncs retry at intervals that grow to one minute, and a "storage unavailable" answer pauses syncing for five minutes ([src/sync.js](../app/src/sync.js)). Its [robots.txt](../app/robots.txt) asks crawlers to keep out of `/audio/`, `/data/` and `/api/`, the guard against crawler spikes the research suggested for `/audio/`.

### 6.2 Files and size: shipping 7,359 recordings

Measured on 2026-11-02, the folder Vercel serves (`app/` without `tests/` and `scripts/`, which [.vercelignore](../app/.vercelignore) leaves out) has **7,441 files and 47.3 MB**. The recordings are 7,359 of these files and 42.4 MB: 1,648 syllables (9.8 KB on average) and 5,711 words (4.6 KB on average) in [app/audio](../app/audio).

**The file-count limit is disputed.** Vercel's Limits page says a deployment made with the command-line tool (CLI) may upload at most 15,000 source files, and that CLI uploads are limited to 100 MB on Hobby ([Vercel Limits](https://vercel.com/docs/limits)). A changelog post of 17 June 2026, "CLI deployment limits removed", says Vercel removed "CLI-specific deployment limits" but does not say which ([Vercel changelog](https://vercel.com/changelog/cli-deployment-limits-removed)). The current CLI (version 65.9.0, 31 October 2026) still answers a server error called `too_many_files` with the advice to upload one archive using `--archive=tgz` ([Vercel CLI 65.9.0 code](https://registry.npmjs.org/vercel/-/vercel-65.9.0.tgz)). Which reading is current is **UNVERIFIED**. The research found no file-count or size limit for deployments made through the Git integration, and the Limits page sets no upper limit on the files a build produces, only warning that 100,000 or more can push a build past 45 minutes ([Vercel Limits](https://vercel.com/docs/limits)).

**Decision:** deploy through the Git integration and stay under the stricter reading anyway: 7,441 files is about half of 15,000, and 47.3 MB is under 100 MB (the [README](../README.md) keeps this rule). Any deployment made with the CLI uses `vercel deploy --archive=tgz` ([vercel deploy](https://vercel.com/docs/cli/deploy)).

**Deployment storage.** Hobby includes 10 GB. Vercel keeps each project's 3 most recent production deployments and its 3 most recent deployments of any kind, never deletes the current production deployment, and, when a team is over 10 GB, deletes the others at once instead of after 30 days ([Vercel changelog](https://vercel.com/changelog/hobby-projects-now-retain-fewer-deployments-to-free-up-storage)). Six kept deployments of 47.3 MB come to about 0.28 GB, under 3% (arithmetic; it assumes unchanged files are counted again in every deployment, which the research could not establish). Users reported in September 2026 that more deployments were kept than this rule allows and that storage did not drop after deletions ([Vercel Community](https://community.vercel.com/t/hobby-deployment-storage-stuck-at-17-gb-after-deleting-2-3-of-the-projects-deployments/49569)); this section follows the changelog, because it is Vercel's own statement of the rule.

**Vercel Blob was ruled out.** Blob is Vercel's file storage. Hobby includes 1 GB of it but only 2,000 "advanced operations" a month, and every upload is one; going over blocks Blob until 30 days have passed ([Vercel Blob pricing](https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-blob/usage-and-pricing)). Uploading the 7,359 recordings would take 3.7 months of allowance (arithmetic).

**Caching.** Vercel's default header for a file is `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, which tells browsers to check with the server before reusing a copy. Vercel's own CDN still caches static files; each deployment has its own cache, and a new deployment clears it ([Vercel Cache-Control headers](https://vercel.com/docs/caching/cache-control-headers), [Vercel caching](https://vercel.com/docs/concepts/edge-network/caching)). Headers can be set per path in `vercel.json` ([vercel.json reference](https://vercel.com/docs/project-configuration/vercel-json)). The research suggested long-lived caching for files whose names change with their content, and a check on every load for `index.html`, the manifest and `sw.js`. What [app/vercel.json](../app/vercel.json) sets:

| Path | Cache-Control | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `/`, `/index.html`, `/sw.js`, `/sw-logic.js`, `/sw-precache.js` | `no-cache` | the browser checks for a new version every time |
| `/data/*` | `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` | the app asks for `data/words-1.json?v=<content hash>`, and the service worker saves each new version itself, keeping only the newest (6.4) |
| `/audio/*` | `public, max-age=604800` (7 days) | recordings are named by word or syllable, not by content (6.4) |
| everything else, including the web app manifest | Vercel's default, `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` | the check on every load the research advised for the manifest |

### 6.3 Sync storage: Upstash Redis, not Neon

**Vercel's own databases are gone.** "Vercel KV is no longer available": existing stores were moved to Upstash Redis in December 2024, Vercel Postgres databases were moved to Neon, and new projects install a database from the Vercel Marketplace ([Redis on Vercel](https://vercel.com/docs/redis), [Postgres on Vercel](https://vercel.com/docs/postgres)). The npm packages `@vercel/kv` and `@vercel/postgres` carry matching deprecation notices ([@vercel/kv](https://registry.npmjs.org/@vercel%2fkv/latest), [@vercel/postgres](https://registry.npmjs.org/@vercel%2fpostgres/latest)), and Neon announced a phased migration starting in November 2024 ([Neon blog source](https://github.com/neondatabase/website/blob/8bfdafd187bddf2eb4c721ecf368fa6d05895878/content/blog/posts/leveling-up-our-partnership-with-vercel.md)). The month "December 2024" comes from Vercel's pages as read during the research; it fits a migration that began in November.

**Upstash Redis, free plan** ([Upstash billing docs](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/overall/billing.mdx), repository state of 30 October 2026):

| | Free plan |
| --- | --- |
| Price | \$0 a month |
| Data | 250 MB |
| Commands | 500,000 a month, no daily cap |
| Bandwidth | 10 GB a month |
| Largest request / largest single value | 10 MB / 100 MB |
| Databases | 1 per account |

- Commands over the free limit fail with an error ("The exceeding commands return exception") instead of being billed ([Upstash FAQ](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/help/faq.mdx)).
- Eviction is off by default, so writes are refused once the data is full ([Upstash eviction](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/features/eviction.mdx)); persistence is always on ([Upstash durability](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/features/durability.mdx)).
- A free database is archived after at least 30 days without activity, after several warning emails. The data is backed up and can be restored into a new database from the Upstash console ([Upstash FAQ](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/help/faq.mdx)). What counts as activity is not documented.
- Adding a payment method moves every free resource on the Upstash account to a paid plan ([Upstash billing docs](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/overall/billing.mdx)).

Two figures disagree between Upstash's own pages. An older documentation page gives 256 MB of data ([Upstash pricing and limits, older page](https://upstash.com/docs/redis/overall/pricing)), while the billing page and the pricing page give 250 MB ([Upstash pricing](https://upstash.com/pricing/redis)); this section uses 250 MB, the newer figure from the primary page. The FAQ still has a heading about a free limit of "10.000 requests per day", left over from an earlier free plan ([Upstash FAQ](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/help/faq.mdx)); the current billing page lists 500,000 commands a month with no daily cap, and that is used.

**Connecting it.** The Marketplace integration creates (or links) an Upstash account and gives the project the database's address and token as environment variables; the app must be redeployed before it sees them ([Upstash's Vercel guide](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/howto/vercelintegration.mdx)). Databases created this way use the names `KV_REST_API_URL` and `KV_REST_API_TOKEN` ([Upstash on the Vercel Marketplace](https://vercel.com/marketplace/upstash/upstash-kv)), which Upstash's official client also reads ([@upstash/redis 1.40.0 code](https://registry.npmjs.org/@upstash/redis/-/redis-1.40.0.tgz)). No client library is needed: Upstash's REST API takes each command as a JSON array with a Bearer token ([Upstash REST API](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/features/restapi.mdx)). Keys and tokens belong in Vercel "Secret" environment variables, which cannot be read back after saving ([Vercel environment variables](https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables/sensitive-environment-variables)).

**The alternative, Neon (Postgres).** Free plan: \$0, 1 GB of storage per project, 100 CU-hours (compute-unit hours) per project a month and 5 GB of transfer. The database sleeps after 5 minutes without queries (on the free plan this cannot be switched off) and wakes within a few hundred milliseconds. Over a limit, the compute is suspended or writes fail, but no data is deleted ([Neon plans](https://github.com/neondatabase/website/blob/8bfdafd187bddf2eb4c721ecf368fa6d05895878/content/docs/introduction/plans.md), [Neon scale to zero](https://github.com/neondatabase/website/blob/8bfdafd187bddf2eb4c721ecf368fa6d05895878/content/docs/introduction/scale-to-zero.md)).

**Decision: Upstash Redis through the Marketplace.** Each account's data is a few JSON records, read and written by key, which is what Redis does; Neon would add an SQL schema and a database driver and gain nothing for this data. The reference project already runs on Upstash, and Upstash's free plan allows one database per account ([Upstash billing docs](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/overall/billing.mdx)), so both apps can share one database. Neon stays the fallback if the app ever needs real queries.

**In the app today** (details in [docs/SYNC.md](SYNC.md)):

- [api/_lib/store.js](../app/api/_lib/store.js) calls Upstash's REST API with plain `fetch`, using `KV_REST_API_URL` / `KV_REST_API_TOKEN` (or Upstash's own `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_*` names); the app has no npm dependencies. Shared code sits in `api/_lib/`, which Vercel does not turn into endpoints because the folder name starts with an underscore ([@vercel/fs-detectors code](https://registry.npmjs.org/@vercel/fs-detectors/-/fs-detectors-7.11.4.tgz)).
- Sign-in uses the shared accounts service of the reference project, the Inburgering flashcards app (`ACCOUNTS_URL`, `ACCOUNTS_SERVICE_KEY`).
- The data model differs from the research sketch of one JSON document per user: each account has one record (stars, skips, settings, statistics) plus its card states split into 32 buckets, so a sync sends only the cards that changed. All keys start with `learn-chinese:`, so the database can be shared.
- Without Redis the API answers "storage unavailable" and the app shows "Sync unavailable"; everything keeps working on the device ([src/sync.js](../app/src/sync.js)). *Settings → Backup* exports and imports a file with all progress ([settings.js](../app/src/views/settings.js)), the safety net for an archived database.

**Will the free plan last?** Arithmetic for a signed-in learner, using the figures in [docs/SYNC.md](SYNC.md): the app syncs 1.5 s after the last answer, so with a pause between cards there is about one sync per answered card and about 5 commands per answered card; every sync reads all 32 buckets, about 120 bytes per studied card. Answers a day come from section 4's simulation ([simulate-plan.mjs](../scripts/simulate-plan.mjs)) for the defaults (10 new words a day, Listen and Speak cards; it leaves out the *In a phrase* cards, which are also on by default for words that occur in a checked phrase).

| Point in time | Answers a day | Studied cards | Commands a month | Data read a month |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| after 3 months | about 184 | about 1,800 | about 27,600 (5.5% of 500,000) | about 1.2 GB |
| after a year | about 259 | about 7,300 | about 38,900 (7.8%) | about 6.8 GB |

The table leaves out the extra reads when the app is reopened. Commands stay small; the data read grows with every card studied. The research did not check how Upstash measures its 10 GB of monthly bandwidth ([Upstash billing docs](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/overall/billing.mdx)). If these reads count, a year of steady study at the default pace uses about two thirds of it, and every app sharing the database shares the allowance. Reading only the buckets that changed would remove most of it (left for later, 6.9).

### 6.4 Offline: the service worker

**Practice.** MDN describes four moments to save files (when the worker installs, as requests pass through, when the user asks, and periodically) and three main strategies: cache first, "cache first with cache refresh" (also called stale-while-revalidate) and network first. Old caches are deleted when a new worker activates ([MDN caching guide](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/progressive_web_apps/guides/caching/index.md)). Audio elements ask for files in byte ranges; Workbox, Google's service-worker library, answers such a request from a whole saved file with a "206 Partial Content" slice ([Workbox range requests](https://github.com/GoogleChrome/workbox/blob/4ea3138a120fd2c87389b54130e25f47e0fd7089/packages/workbox-range-requests/src/RangeRequestsPlugin.ts)), and its offline recipe returns a saved page when a page load fails ([Workbox offline fallback](https://github.com/GoogleChrome/workbox/blob/4ea3138a120fd2c87389b54130e25f47e0fd7089/packages/workbox-recipes/src/offlineFallback.ts)).

**Updates.** Browsers look for a new worker when a page of the app is opened, and when an event reaches the worker and it has not been downloaded for 24 hours. A changed worker installs in the background and waits until no page uses the old one, unless it is told to take over with `skipWaiting()` ([MDN Service Worker API](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/api/service_worker_api/index.md), [MDN skipWaiting](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/api/serviceworkerglobalscope/skipwaiting/index.md)). Vercel clears its cache on every deployment ([Vercel caching](https://vercel.com/docs/concepts/edge-network/caching)), and the app's headers make browsers check `sw.js` on every load ([app/vercel.json](../app/vercel.json)), so a new worker is seen at the next check.

**Decision:** a hand-written "classic" worker without a library. The app and its data are served from the phone first, recordings are saved the first time they play, sign-in and sync always use the network, and a new version is offered with a *Reload* button instead of switching in the middle of a study session. A classic worker (no `import` inside it) is the most compatible kind; workers with `import` need Chrome 91, Safari 15 or Firefox 147 ([MDN compat data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/0efedff8c8b6f131ad029443056bc74e00b1aca2/javascript/statements.json)).

**What was built** ([app/sw.js](../app/sw.js); the rules are in [app/sw-logic.js](../app/sw-logic.js)):

| What | How it is served | Offline |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Opening a page | network first; after 3 seconds, or on a server error, the saved app | the saved app (the app is a single page, so it is its own offline page); on the very first visit there is nothing saved yet |
| App shell: 53 files, 0.5 MB (HTML, CSS, JavaScript, icons, manifest, the data index) | saved when the worker installs, in one cache per version; cache first | yes |
| Word lists and content (`data/*.json`, 4.3 MB) | cache first, by an address that includes a hash of the content (`?v=…`); only the newest copy of each file is kept | yes: after the first online visit the app saves every list in the background, except when Data Saver is on |
| Recordings (`audio/*.mp3`) | cache first; saved whole on first play; byte-range requests answered with a 206 from the saved file | yes, once played, or all at once with *Settings → Offline → Save audio of my active decks for offline use* |
| Sign-in and sync (`api/`) | never handled or saved by the worker | waits; sync tries again when the phone is back online |

When a new version has installed, the app shows "A new version of the app is ready" with *Reload* and *Later*; *Reload* tells the waiting worker to take over. Because an installed app can stay open for days, the app also looks for a new version when it comes back to the screen after 30 minutes or more ([src/main.js](../app/src/main.js)). [scripts/build-sw.mjs](../scripts/build-sw.mjs) writes the list of app-shell files and a hash of their contents into `sw-precache.js`, which the worker loads, and stamps the hash into `sw.js`, so every change to the app gives the worker new bytes. The worker's routes, byte ranges, install, update and offline behaviour are covered by automated tests, including a check that this generated list is up to date ([app/tests/sw.test.mjs](../app/tests/sw.test.mjs)).

Where the build differs from the research plan:

- There is no separate offline page; the saved app answers page loads.
- The research suggested long cache lifetimes for files named by content. For data the worker has the same effect, because each address carries the content hash. Recordings are named by word or syllable, not by content; browsers may keep them for a week ([app/vercel.json](../app/vercel.json)) and the worker keeps them for good, never downloading a saved one again ([app/sw.js](../app/sw.js)). A recording replaced under the same name would therefore stay old on phones that saved it, until the audio cache is renamed.

### 6.5 iPhone and Android: installing, keeping data, sound, background

**Installing.** Chrome and other Chromium browsers install a site that is served over HTTPS and has a web app manifest with a name, icons of 192 and 512 pixels, a start address and a display mode ([MDN: making PWAs installable](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/progressive_web_apps/guides/making_pwas_installable/index.md)). An `id` gives the installed app a fixed identity; without one Chrome derives it from the start address ([Chrome: manifest id](https://github.com/GoogleChrome/developer.chrome.com/blob/51dd7dd5d510ed85d86f5a91cb8fde50b62351c7/site/en/articles/pwa-manifest-id/index.md)), so changing that address later can make an installed app unrecognisable ([web.dev: web app manifest](https://web.dev/learn/pwa/web-app-manifest)).

- **Android:** Chrome (on phones with Google's services) and Samsung Internet (on Samsung phones) create a real installed app; other browsers add a shortcut. A page can show its own *Install* button through the `beforeinstallprompt` event, which Safari and Firefox do not support ([MDN](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/progressive_web_apps/guides/making_pwas_installable/index.md), [MDN compat data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/0efedff8c8b6f131ad029443056bc74e00b1aca2/api/BeforeInstallPromptEvent.json)).
- **iPhone:** there is no install prompt. The user taps *Share*, then *Add to Home Screen*; since iOS 16.4 this also works from Chrome, Edge, Firefox and Orion ([MDN](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/progressive_web_apps/guides/making_pwas_installable/index.md), [Safari 16.4 release notes](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/safari-release-notes/safari-16_4-release-notes.json)). Safari 26.0 (15 September 2025) "added support for any website to become a web app on iOS or iPadOS" ([Safari 26.0 release notes](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/safari-release-notes/safari-26-release-notes.json)). Safari uses an `apple-touch-icon` link in preference to the manifest's icons ([MDN compat data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/0efedff8c8b6f131ad029443056bc74e00b1aca2/manifests/webapp/icons.json)).

Sources disagree on one detail: Chrome's blog says its automatic install prompt still needs a service worker that handles requests, while Google's archived install criteria list no such requirement ([Chrome blog](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/update-install-criteria), [web.dev source](https://github.com/GoogleChrome/web.dev/blob/a64d870f59c25431bbedf0ef06aa635072c43a2e/src/site/content/en/progressive-web-apps/install-criteria/index.md)). It does not matter here, because this app's worker handles requests.

*In the app today:* the manifest has an `id`, the name "Learn Chinese", the short name 中文, a start address and scope, standalone display, and 192, 512 and maskable 512 pixel icons; `index.html` links a 180 × 180 `apple-touch-icon` ([manifest.webmanifest](../app/manifest.webmanifest), [index.html](../app/index.html)). The iPhone instructions are in *Settings → Offline*. There is no *Install* button for Android and no install banner on iPhone ([src/offline.js](../app/src/offline.js)).

**Keeping data.** Space is not the problem; deletion is.

- Chrome lets one site use up to 60% of the disk. Safari 17 and later (iOS 17 and later) allows about 60% in Safari, and a site saved to the Home Screen gets the same quota as in Safari ([MDN storage quotas](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/api/storage_api/storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria/index.md), [WebKit storage policy](https://webkit.org/blog/14403/updates-to-storage-policy/)). The whole app with every recording saved is under 50 MB.
- When a device runs short of space, browsers delete whole sites, least recently used first, and skip sites that were granted persistent storage. A site asks for that with `navigator.storage.persist()`. Chrome and Safari decide silently from how the site is used, WebKit for example favouring Home Screen apps; Firefox asks the user ([MDN storage quotas](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/api/storage_api/storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria/index.md), [WebKit storage policy](https://webkit.org/blog/14403/updates-to-storage-policy/)).
- Safari also deletes everything a site's scripts have stored (localStorage, IndexedDB, service worker data) once the user has used Safari on 7 days without interacting with that site ([MDN storage quotas](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/api/storage_api/storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria/index.md), [WebKit tracking prevention](https://webkit.org/tracking-prevention/)). A Home Screen app keeps its own count of days of use ([WebKit blog](https://webkit.org/blog/10218/full-third-party-cookie-blocking-and-more/)), and its own website is exempt from this deletion; WebKit's current source code still says so ("This can be home screen web applications", [WebKit source](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/ee10f6908b65c932143827e6e5dc5992733bfb2c/Source/WebKit/NetworkProcess/Classifier/ResourceLoadStatisticsStore.cpp)).
- localStorage holds at most 5 MiB per site ([MDN storage quotas](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/api/storage_api/storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria/index.md)).

**Decision:** on iPhone, use the app from the Home Screen; ask for persistent storage after real use, not on the first visit; and keep a second copy of the progress on the server or in a backup file.

*In the app today:* progress is kept in localStorage ([storage.js](../app/src/lib/storage.js)). The app asks for persistent storage after the first answer of a study session, and *Settings → Offline* shows the space used, the browser's quota and a *Keep data* button ([study.js](../app/src/views/study.js), [offline.js](../app/src/offline.js)). At about 120 bytes per card state ([docs/SYNC.md](SYNC.md)), 5 MiB holds about 44,000 cards (arithmetic): years at the default pace, but not the whole deck of up to about 50,000 cards ([src/sync.js](../app/src/sync.js)).

The app also tells iPhone users that the Home Screen app may not see what was saved in Safari (progress and recordings) ([offline.js](../app/src/offline.js)). Whether the two share storage on iOS 26 and 27 could not be confirmed; it is **UNVERIFIED**. The advice that comes with it (sign in, or export a backup, and save the audio again inside the installed app) is safe either way.

**Sound.**

- Browsers block sound that starts without a user action, and `play()` then fails with an error called NotAllowedError ([MDN autoplay guide](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/media/guides/autoplay/index.md)).
- The HTML standard fixes what counts as a user action: a key press, a mouse button press, lifting a finger (`touchend`, or `pointerup` from a touch), and the `click` that follows them. Touching down (`touchstart`, or `pointerdown` from a touch) does not count ([HTML standard source](https://github.com/whatwg/html/blob/404b3eb14a7a936dddefb2d78e224dbeb01d2358/source)), so playback should start in a click handler. (The second pass corrected this: the first draft listed `pointerdown` as enough.)
- **The Silent switch.** While an `<audio>` element plays, WebKit puts the page in iOS's "playback" audio category, which keeps playing with the switch set to silent. A page that uses only Web Audio gets the "ambient" category, which the switch silences ([WebKit source](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/ee10f6908b65c932143827e6e5dc5992733bfb2c/Source/WebCore/platform/audio/cocoa/MediaSessionManagerCocoa.mm), [Apple: playback](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/avfaudio/avaudiosession/category-swift.struct/playback.json), [Apple: ambient](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/avfaudio/avaudiosession/category-swift.struct/ambient.json)). A page can override this with `navigator.audioSession.type` (Safari 16.4 and later, not Chrome) ([MDN compat data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/0efedff8c8b6f131ad029443056bc74e00b1aca2/api/AudioSession.json)); the type "playback" stops other apps' sound ([MDN Audio Session API](https://github.com/mdn/content/blob/a0ba038b5e77c06c820e90dc39133acb70dd476a/files/en-us/web/api/audio_session_api/index.md)).
- Safari 26.2 fixed an `<audio>` element that failed to play after a Home Screen app was reopened ([Safari 26.2 release notes](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/safari-release-notes/safari-26_2-release-notes.json)).

*In the app today:* recordings play through a single `<audio>` element, started from the click handlers of the *Play* buttons ([audio.js](../app/src/audio.js), [study.js](../app/src/views/study.js)), so by the rules above they should play with the Silent switch on. Record-and-compare uses Web Audio and sets the audio session to "play-and-record" while recording and to "playback" afterwards ([recorder.js](../app/src/recorder.js); see section 5). With *Autoplay* on (the default), a listen card's recording starts once the next card has loaded, which is no longer directly inside the tap; whether an iPhone always allows that has not been tested (**UNVERIFIED**). If playback is refused, the app speaks the word with the device's voice instead. How the device voice behaves with the Silent switch was not part of this research.

**Nothing runs in the background.** Background Sync, Periodic Background Sync and Background Fetch exist only in Chromium browsers (from Chrome 49, 80 and 74); Safari and Firefox have none ([MDN compat data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/0efedff8c8b6f131ad029443056bc74e00b1aca2/api/SyncManager.json)), and no Safari release from 26.0 to 27.2 (26 October 2026, the latest) added them ([Safari release notes](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/safari-release-notes/safari-27_2-release-notes.json), [release notes index](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/safari-release-notes.json)). Push notifications work on iPhone only for Home Screen apps (iOS 16.4 and later), with permission asked in response to a tap ([MDN compat data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/0efedff8c8b6f131ad029443056bc74e00b1aca2/api/PushManager.json), [WebKit: Web Push](https://webkit.org/blog/13878/web-push-for-web-apps-on-ios-and-ipados/)).

**Decision:** never depend on background work; sync while the app is open. *In the app today:* when the learner is signed in, the app syncs when it starts, 1.5 s after a change, when it comes back to the screen (at most every 30 s), with a small final request when it is hidden, and when the phone comes back online ([src/sync.js](../app/src/sync.js)). There are no push notifications in this version; study reminders would only work on an iPhone for the Home Screen app.

**The Netherlands (EU).** During the iOS 17.4 betas in February 2024, Apple is reported to have planned to remove Home Screen web apps in the EU, citing the Digital Markets Act, and to have reversed this around 1 March 2024. This is **UNVERIFIED**: no primary source could be reached. Verified: Safari 17.4 was released on 5 March 2024 ([Safari release notes index](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/safari-release-notes.json)); Apple's current page on its EU changes under the Digital Markets Act does not mention Home Screen web apps ([Apple Developer](https://developer.apple.com/support/apps-in-the-eu)); and Safari 26.0's "any website can become a web app" names no regional limit ([Safari 26.0 release notes](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/safari-release-notes/safari-26-release-notes.json)). The working assumption, that Home Screen web apps work in the Netherlands as elsewhere, is an inference and also **UNVERIFIED**. One test on the learner's iPhone settles it, and the app stays fully usable in a Safari tab.

### 6.6 No framework and no build step

**Decision: keep plain HTML, CSS and native ES modules, with no bundler or build step.** The research checked each reason a build might become necessary and found none:

1. **The service worker** is a plain file (6.4).
2. **ts-fsrs**, the scheduler library, needs no build in either version (sizes, dates and browser support are in 4.2): version 5.4.2 ([npm registry](https://registry.npmjs.org/ts-fsrs)) ships `dist/index.mjs` as one self-contained ES module that can be copied into the app as it is ([ts-fsrs 5.4.2 package](https://registry.npmjs.org/ts-fsrs/-/ts-fsrs-5.4.2.tgz)); version 6.0.0 (FSRS-7 by default, breaking changes) imports `@open-spaced-repetition/srs-kit` by its bare name ([ts-fsrs 6.0.0 package](https://registry.npmjs.org/ts-fsrs/-/ts-fsrs-6.0.0.tgz), [srs-kit](https://registry.npmjs.org/@open-spaced-repetition%2fsrs-kit)), so without a bundler it would need an import map ([MDN compat data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/0efedff8c8b6f131ad029443056bc74e00b1aca2/html/elements/script.json)) plus copied srs-kit files, or its all-in-one bundle. Which FSRS version to use is decided in section 4.
3. **Many data files:** the scripts that generate them can also write the worker's file list, so the site itself needs no build.
4. **Vercel serves such a site.** With Framework Preset "Other" and an empty Build Command, files are served as they are, from `public/` if that folder exists and otherwise from the project folder ([Vercel: configuring a build](https://vercel.com/docs/builds/configure-a-build), [@vercel/frameworks code](https://registry.npmjs.org/@vercel/frameworks/-/frameworks-3.35.0.tgz)). Every `.js` file in `api/` becomes a function, except paths with a part starting with `_` or `.` ([@vercel/fs-detectors code](https://registry.npmjs.org/@vercel/fs-detectors/-/fs-detectors-7.11.4.tgz)). Without a framework, ES-module functions need `"type": "module"` in `package.json` ([Vercel Node.js runtime](https://vercel.com/docs/functions/runtimes/node-js)).

Vercel currently offers Node.js 26.x, 24.x and 22.x; 20.x was discontinued on 1 October 2026 ([@vercel/build-utils code](https://registry.npmjs.org/@vercel/build-utils/-/build-utils-15.2.1.tgz)). One copy of Vercel's documentation still showed 24.x as the default with 20.x available, another 26.x as the default ([Vercel: Node.js versions](https://vercel.com/docs/functions/runtimes/node-js/node-js-versions)); the build package is Vercel's own current code, so its list is used.

A build would become worth it for TypeScript or JSX, for npm packages that only work through bare-name imports, or for a code base large enough to need minification.

*In the app today:* [app/package.json](../app/package.json) declares ES modules and has no dependencies, and each function exports a `POST` handler ([api/sync.js](../app/api/sync.js)). On 2026-11-02 the copied [ts-fsrs.mjs](../app/src/vendor/ts-fsrs.mjs) was compared with `dist/index.mjs` from the published 5.4.2 package; that file's SHA-256 checksum `ad4a4b3b7e259fcbf02764454c8f9db4ea3bf5aae2f473198129ecb7728f1a19` matches the research ([ts-fsrs 5.4.2 package](https://registry.npmjs.org/ts-fsrs/-/ts-fsrs-5.4.2.tgz)). The two are identical apart from a four-line header and a removed source-map comment; the licence is copied next to it. As the research recommends, `app/package.json` pins `"node": "24.x"`, a version Vercel offers; and the update script named in the header, [scripts/vendor-ts-fsrs.mjs](../scripts/vendor-ts-fsrs.mjs), re-creates the copy from the pinned version after checking the download against the checksum npm publishes for it.

### 6.7 Deployment through GitHub

**Why the agent cannot deploy.** The cloud sandbox in which the app was built reaches GitHub and npm, but its network proxy refuses vercel.com, api.vercel.com and vercel.app with "403" (re-tested on 2026-11-02 in [the sandbox's proxy status](http://127.0.0.1:38659/__agentproxy/status), a local address that only works inside that sandbox). So the Vercel command-line tool cannot deploy from there, and a deployed page cannot be tested from there. Pushing to the project's GitHub repository works: git goes through Anthropic's GitHub proxy, which allows pushes of branches ([Claude Code: cloud environments](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments.md), [the repository](https://github.com/OOC7117/learn-chinese)).

**Decision: Vercel's Git integration.** The learner does this once (also in the [README](../README.md) and [docs/SYNC.md](SYNC.md)):

1. Create a Vercel Hobby account, choose *Add New → Project* and import `OOC7117/learn-chinese`. Set *Root Directory* to `app` and *Framework Preset* to *Other*, with no Build Command and no Output Directory (switch on *Override* and leave the Build Command empty) ([Vercel: configuring a build](https://vercel.com/docs/builds/configure-a-build)).
2. Check which branch is the *Production Branch*. On 2026-11-02 the repository has a single branch, `claude/chinese-learning-repo-vaelpt`.
3. Optional, for sign-in and sync: in the project's *Storage* tab add *Upstash for Redis* from the Marketplace (pick an EU region if offered) and connect it; add the environment variables `ACCOUNTS_URL` and, as a Secret, `ACCOUNTS_SERVICE_KEY`; redeploy ([Upstash's Vercel guide](https://github.com/upstash/docs/blob/93c9c79f1fe75192bd104c560658820f3be5ab8e/redis/howto/vercelintegration.mdx), [Vercel environment variables](https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables/sensitive-environment-variables)).

After that, every push to GitHub starts a deployment; Git-started deployments, previews included, count toward the 100 a day ([Vercel Community](https://community.vercel.com/t/being-rate-limited-on-hobby-for-deployment-limits-but-im-well-under-100/49793)).

**If the learner wants the agent to deploy and test by itself:** in the cloud environment's settings, set *Network access* to *Custom*, allow vercel.com, api.vercel.com and `*.vercel.app`, and keep the default list of package managers ticked. Then store a Vercel access token either as a network secret for api.vercel.com, which the proxy adds to requests so the token never enters the sandbox (offered on the Pro, Max, Ultra, Team and Enterprise plans), or as the environment variable `VERCEL_TOKEN`, which anyone using the environment can read ([Claude Code: cloud environments](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments.md)). Not tested: whether the proxy's token works when the Vercel tool also sends its own `Authorization` header. Either way, installing Upstash from the Marketplace needs a person to accept its terms in a browser; only Neon's integration can accept them from the command line ([Vercel CLI changelog](https://github.com/vercel/vercel/blob/84878e532142f0ac584953695658b324eb9e5b80/packages/cli/CHANGELOG.md)).

### 6.8 The Claude API: not used by the app

The app calls no AI service. Its Content-Security-Policy allows network connections only to its own site (`connect-src 'self'` in [app/vercel.json](../app/vercel.json)), and no file in `app/` or `scripts/` calls the Claude API (searched on 2026-11-02). AI agents researched and arranged the content during development; [docs/AI-CONTENT.md](AI-CONTENT.md) lists what they did.

For a possible later feature on the server, the research recorded:

- The API key belongs on the server only. Anthropic's TypeScript SDK refuses to run in a browser unless forced, "to avoid exposing your secret API credentials" ([SDK README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-typescript/main/README.md)), and reads the key from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` ([Claude: get started](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/get-started.md)); on Vercel it would be a Secret environment variable ([Vercel environment variables](https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables/sensitive-environment-variables)).
- Prices per million tokens, input / output: Claude Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`) \$4 / \$20, Claude Sonnet 5.5 \$2 / \$10, Claude Haiku 5.5 \$0.10 / \$0.50 for prompts up to 100,000 tokens; the Batch API halves them; the tokens a model spends thinking are billed as output ([Claude pricing](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing.md), [Claude models overview](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/overview.md), [Claude thinking](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/thinking.md)).
- Any Chinese a model wrote would have to pass the same dictionary check and native-speaker review as everything else before the app showed it.

### 6.9 What waits, and why

| Waits | Why | What settles it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The first deployment, and a look at the live site on both phones | the sandbox cannot reach Vercel (6.7) | the learner's one-time import; it also confirms that "no build plus `api/` functions" works, which the documentation does not spell out |
| The Production Branch | the repository has only `claude/chinese-learning-repo-vaelpt` (6.7) | choose it, or a `main` branch, when importing |
| Re-reading Vercel's plan figures | the second pass could not open vercel.com (6.1, 6.2) | vercel.com, or the documentation search of Vercel's connector for Claude |
| The CLI file and size limits | disputed (6.2) | nothing for Git deployments; a CLI deployment uses `--archive=tgz` |
| Functions per deployment on Hobby, and the functions' region | not checked; the app has five functions in [app/api](../app/api) | Vercel's limits page; an EU region for the Upstash database when offered |
| Upstash reads per sync | every sync reads all 32 buckets; after a year at the default pace about 6.8 GB a month, if counted (6.3) | Upstash's usage page; make a sync read only the buckets that changed |
| Upstash archiving after 30 days without activity | what counts as activity is not documented (6.3) | export a backup before a long break; a daily Vercel cron job, which Hobby allows once a day ([Vercel cron limits](https://vercel.com/docs/cron-jobs/usage-and-pricing)), might keep it active |
| iPhone checks | Silent switch with word audio and record-and-compare; listen-card autoplay; byte-range playback from the cache; Safari versus Home Screen storage; the Home Screen app in the Netherlands (6.5) | one session on the learner's iPhone |
| Install prompts | no *Install* button on Android; the iPhone instructions are only in *Settings* (6.5) | a `beforeinstallprompt` button; a one-time hint in Safari, hidden in the installed app |
| What installed apps see while the project is paused | not known what Vercel answers then; the worker shows the saved app only on a network error, a timeout or a server error (6.4) | check Vercel's answer for a paused project; widen the fallback if needed |
| Card states beyond localStorage | 5 MiB is about 44,000 card states (6.5) | move them to IndexedDB before a learner gets near that |
| A recording replaced under the same name | phones that saved it keep the old one (6.4) | rename the audio cache |
| Study reminders | push works on iPhone only in the Home Screen app (6.5) | a later version |

## Open questions

Collected from the sections above and from the open questions of the 20 research files, with duplicates merged. Your own decisions come first. The engineering work that waits stays in the sections' own lists ([1.10](#110-recommendation-what-to-learn-and-how-every-word-is-tagged), [2.1](#21-dictionary-cc-cedict) to [2.6](#26-pinyin-tooling), [3.10](#310-what-is-hidden-and-what-is-left-for-later), [4.2](#42-the-library-ts-fsrs-542-fsrs-6-now-fsrs-7-later), [4.5](#45-the-design-that-follows), [5.5](#55-what-waits-and-why), [6.9](#69-what-waits-and-why)).

### Your decisions

1. **Which forms of address the family expects.** Before marriage, 叔叔/阿姨 for your partner's parents, or 伯父/伯母 if they are older than your own parents; for the mother's parents, 外公/外婆, 姥爷/姥姥 or a local word; and the family's own words for other relatives (such as 娭毑 or 爹爹), how relatives are numbered (排行) and how younger relatives and in-laws are addressed, which the research could only infer. When to change to 爸/妈 (改口) is usually the wedding, unless the parents invite it earlier. The app teaches both standard pairs; ask your partner which ones the family uses ([3.4](#34-family), [3.6](#36-the-etiquette-that-matters-most), [3.7](#37-regional-notes-for-a-family-from-south-and-central-china)).
2. **How many new words a day, at which retention.** The default is 10 with Listen + Speak, about 17 minutes a day after a month and 35 after a year in the model; 5 a day roughly halves that, and Lighter (0.85) saves about a tenth to a fifth. The model assumes 8 seconds per answer and speaking aloud takes longer, so compare it with your real study time on the Progress page after a few weeks. Before you start, decide how many HSK 1–2 words to mark as known with "Sort: know / learn" ([4.5](#45-the-design-that-follows), [4.6](#46-a-realistic-plan)).
3. **Whether to pay for generated audio.** Replacing the device voice with Google's voices for the 4,983 words without a recording and for every phrase, kinship term and example costs US\$0 within the monthly free tier (about US\$0.11 at the WaveNet list price), but it needs a Google Cloud project with billing enabled and an API key, a choice between WaveNet and Chirp 3: HD, and first a listening test of 20 to 30 items with your partner ([2.3](#23-audio)).
4. **Whether to sit the new HSK, and when.** Its first regular sitting is on 13 December 2026. The registration deadlines (16 November on paper, 3 December internet-based) are **UNVERIFIED**, so check CTI's test-dates page first; HSK 3 and above include a speaking paper ([1.5](#15-the-exam-on-2-november-2026), [1.10](#110-recommendation-what-to-learn-and-how-every-word-is-tagged)).
5. **Reading and writing.** When to switch on the Characters cards (the new sample papers print pinyin on HSK 1 and 2 but not on HSK 3 and 4), and whether you want handwriting practice (a stroke-order quiz) or only to watch stroke order; that decides whether and how stroke data is added ([1.10](#110-recommendation-what-to-learn-and-how-every-word-is-tagged), [2.4](#24-characters-stroke-order), [4.5](#45-the-design-that-follows)).
6. **Sign-in, sync and deployment.** Whether to connect the optional sync (Upstash Redis, shared with the reference project) or rely on backup files; which branch Vercel treats as production when you import the repository; and whether an agent may deploy and test by itself, which needs network access to Vercel and a token ([6.3](#63-sync-storage-upstash-redis-not-neon), [6.7](#67-deployment-through-github), [6.9](#69-what-waits-and-why)).
7. **Will the app ever be used commercially?** Vercel's free plan allows only non-commercial personal use, and the answer also decides whether sources licensed for non-commercial use only (Tone Perfect, Tatoeba recordings under such licences) could ever be used, and how the CC BY-SA credits are shown ([2.2](#22-example-sentences), [2.3](#23-audio), [6.1](#61-vercel-hobby-terms-allowances-and-what-happens-at-a-limit)).
8. **Your family's voices in the tone drills.** Recordings of your partner and family would add the extra voices the research suggests, but whether regional accents help or confuse a beginner is not known, nor whether such recordings should then be labelled and kept out of the standard-Mandarin drills at first ([4.4](#44-what-the-evidence-says-about-learning-words-and-tones), [4.5](#45-the-design-that-follows)).

### For your partner, or another native speaker

- Read the 36 AI-assembled dialogues in [AI-CONTENT.md](AI-CONTENT.md) and set `reviewed: true` on the ones that sound natural ([3.8](#38-practice-dialogues)).
- Read the tips with medium or low confidence (most family etiquette rests on informal web sources) and the phrases that were adapted from a sourced sentence or assembled from attested parts: 18 travel phrases, a number of meeting, scheduling and writing phrases, and the safety phrase 我对花生严重过敏, which rests on Tatoeba alone ([3.2](#32-travel), [3.5](#35-work), [3.6](#36-the-etiquette-that-matters-most)).
- Confirm the three readings the app corrects in the 2025 list: 任性 rènxìng, 温差 wēnchā, 脚踏实地 jiǎotàshídì ([1.9](#19-pinyin-checked-against-cc-cedict)).
- Listen to the native word recordings (5,711, none checked yet) and, before audio is generated, to a test set: characters with several readings (行, 长, 了, 觉, 数), two third tones (你好, 可以), 一 and 不, erhua (哪儿, 一会儿), ü syllables, and 拜拜, which must come out as báibái or bāibāi, never bàibài; after generating, the 132 clips with a forced reading and a sample of the rest ([2.3](#23-audio)).
- Listen to your tones now and then: self-grading in the app is for remembering, not for tone accuracy ([4.4](#44-what-the-evidence-says-about-learning-words-and-tones)).
- Explain the family customs the research could not settle: the usual red-envelope sum and who gets one, who gives the 改口费 and how much, who pays when you eat out with the parents, which New Year customs the family keeps (food, visiting order, taboos), whether gifts are opened in front of the giver, how hard drinks are pressed, and how hot "mild" (微辣) is in their province ([3.2](#32-travel), [3.4](#34-family), [3.6](#36-the-etiquette-that-matters-most), [3.7](#37-regional-notes-for-a-family-from-south-and-central-china)).

### Facts to re-check before relying on them

- **Visa-free entry** for Dutch passports ends at 24:00 Beijing time on 31 December 2026 unless it is extended: check the [embassy's notices](https://nl.china-embassy.gov.cn/eng/ls/zytz/) in November and December 2026, and ask the embassy whether a stay that starts in December may run into 2027 ([3.3](#33-travel-facts-that-change-checked-2-november-2026)).
- **The HSK calendar**: the registration deadlines for 13 December, whether HSK 2.0 is offered after that date, whether the standalone HSKK continues, the validity of certificates and trial reports, the HSK 7–9 sittings (and whether the 22 November sitting already uses the 91-item paper), the score scales and pass marks of the new levels, and which version of the 2025 syllabus PDF the official site serves; [CTI's test dates](https://www.chinesetest.cn/test-dates) is the page to check ([1.3](#13-the-2025-hsk-exam-syllabus), [1.5](#15-the-exam-on-2-november-2026)).
- **The 2027 public holidays**, which the State Council usually publishes in November; the festival dates in the app are computed ([3.6](#36-the-etiquette-that-matters-most)).
- **Paying**: the Alipay and WeChat Pay limits before and after passport verification (sources conflict), whether Dutch debit cards link and what Dutch banks charge, PayPal for European accounts, Alipay's TourCard, and restaurant service charges ([3.2](#32-travel), [3.3](#33-travel-facts-that-change-checked-2-november-2026)).
- **Travel practicalities**: passports at automatic station gates (it varies by station), taxi receipts, hotel deposits for foreign guests and DiDi's English app, what the online arrival card asks for when you stay with family, whether 122 and 112 work, the Dutch travel advice before each trip, and the law articles and the 2026 State Council provisions, read only through GitHub copies and search summaries, on an official site ([3.3](#33-travel-facts-that-change-checked-2-november-2026)).
- **Whether the app's address can be reached from mainland China**: GFWList lists vercel.app, so install the app and save the audio before you leave, or test from inside China ([3.3](#33-travel-facts-that-change-checked-2-november-2026)).
- **Vercel and Upstash figures**: the Hobby allowances and terms (re-read once vercel.com can be opened), the CLI file and size limits, whether unchanged files count again in each deployment's storage, functions per deployment and their region, what an installed app sees while a project is paused, what counts as activity for Upstash's 30-day archiving, and whether sync reads count toward its 10 GB of monthly bandwidth ([6.1](#61-vercel-hobby-terms-allowances-and-what-happens-at-a-limit), [6.2](#62-files-and-size-shipping-7359-recordings), [6.3](#63-sync-storage-upstash-redis-not-neon), [6.9](#69-what-waits-and-why)).

### Tests on your phones

- **iPhone**: word audio and record-and-compare with the Silent switch on; listen-card autoplay; audio played from the offline cache (byte ranges); whether the Home Screen app sees what Safari saved; whether Home Screen web apps work normally in the Netherlands (the 2024 EU episode is **UNVERIFIED**); whether the Home Screen app remembers the microphone permission; and speech recognition in a Safari tab and in the Home Screen app on iOS 26/27 ([5.1](#51-speech-recognition-in-the-browser-web-speech-api), [5.3](#53-pitch-tracking-in-the-app), [6.5](#65-iphone-and-android-installing-keeping-data-sound-background)).
- **Android**: where Chrome's speech recognition processes Mandarin, and whether the installed app behaves like a tab ([5.1](#51-speech-recognition-in-the-browser-web-speech-api)).
- **Both**: how accurate the pitch line is on real voices (a small labelled set of your own recordings, third and fourth tones in particular), how much the phone's voice processing bends it, and whether recording and speech recognition can run at the same time ([5.3](#53-pitch-tracking-in-the-app), [5.5](#55-what-waits-and-why)).
- **After the first deployment**: that the site and its `api/` functions work without a build step; later, ts-fsrs 6 (FSRS-7) on the oldest phone the app should support ([4.2](#42-the-library-ts-fsrs-542-fsrs-6-now-fsrs-7-later), [6.9](#69-what-waits-and-why)).

### Research still open

- **Learning method**: the 13 unverified method findings (among them recall against recognition, words alone or in sentences, growing or equal gaps, shadowing, listening before speaking and self-assessment) and the disputed question of voices: how many is "moderate", and can good synthetic voices count as extra voices; whether adaptive scheduling improves learning and not only prediction (Lindsey and colleagues, 2014); the pooled effects in the 2024 and 2025 meta-analyses; how many 3- to 5-minute phone sessions match the eight sessions of the 1999 study; when to start characters; what experienced teachers agree on ([4.4](#44-what-the-evidence-says-about-learning-words-and-tones)); and the original SM-2 text, which could not be read ([4.1](#41-the-scheduler-fsrs-not-sm-2)).
- **Speech scoring**: whether a wrong tone lowers Azure's zh-CN accuracy score, whether Azure returns a prosody score for zh-CN, its price in an EU region and its token lifetime; whether an EU customer can use Tencent's SOE, and what its tone value −1 means; tone scores, EU availability and prices of iFlytek and SpeechSuper; a GDPR review before any voice upload; and controlled evidence that seeing your own pitch line helps adult learners of Mandarin ([5.2](#52-cloud-pronunciation-scoring), [5.3](#53-pitch-tracking-in-the-app)).
- **Audio**: whether Chirp 3: HD honours pinyin phoneme tags, whether the engines re-apply tone changes to pinyin in a phoneme tag, how erhua and ü are written there, and the exact cmn-CN voice names; Azure and Amazon Polly prices and terms; the licence version of Yue Tan's word recordings; Tone Perfect's licence and the Wikimedia Commons and Lingua Libre recordings; which reading each single-character recording holds; whether Google will retire the WaveNet voices, or counts Chirp 3: HD as generative AI under its terms; and whether krmanik's 2025 audio, partly a voice clone of an audio-cmn speaker, could ever be used ([2.3](#23-audio)).
